## Current Phase

**Current session note (2026-06-19 — personal site repository cloned and README published):** Cloned `ObjectInSpace/objectinspace.github.io` into `objectinspace.github.io\`. Updated its `README.md` with Drew Mochak's name and links to a repository-hosted vCard, email, and calendar; copied `C:\Users\amock\Downloads\drew.vcf` into the repository as `drew.vcf`. Committed as `88e1965` (`Add contact links and vCard`) and pushed to `origin/main`. The Date Everything accessibility mod and its pending navigation coverage-sweep proof were not changed.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — offline route validator added and first planner geometry failure found):** User reran the coverage sweep and reported all routes are failing in a new way, then asked whether planner geometry can be validated offline before in-game proof. Added `scripts\validate_offline_routes.py` with two modes: `routes` validates existing route JSON artifacts such as `artifacts\navigation\sweep\default`, and `objects` plans exported interactables through `scripts\plan_object_route.py` and validates the fresh routes. Checks include waypoint-on-navigable-cell, sampled same-floor segment traversal against `navigable_region.bake.json`, and exported blocker-footprint intersections from `thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json`. The script exits nonzero on failures unless `--warn-only` is passed. Initial evidence: `uv run python .\scripts\validate_offline_routes.py routes --max-routes 20 --warn-only` found existing sweep route artifacts stale against the current bake (`187` waypoint-not-navigable and `307` segment-crosses-blocked-cell failures in the first 20 routes). Fresh planning also fails geometry validation: `uv run python .\scripts\validate_offline_routes.py objects --max-objects 5 --warn-only` found `10` segment-crosses-blocked-cell failures, and `uv run python .\scripts\validate_offline_routes.py objects --max-objects 1` correctly exits `1`, with target `12018` crossing blocked cells on smoothed route segments. Likely next fix lane: replace the planner's current Bresenham-style `_segment_is_clear(...)` in `scripts\plan_object_route.py` with the same conservative/sampled or supercover segment check used by the validator, then regenerate sweep artifacts and rerun offline validation before another in-game sweep.

**Clarification follow-up (2026-05-25 — camera zones are diagnostic annotations, not hard blockers yet):** User raised that CameraSpaces could be related to blocker nature. Source review: `CameraSpaces.CameraLoc(Transform t)` uses AABB containment for the player transform or active object collider closest point; `RefreshCamRegions()` prefers active object location before player location. Therefore camera zones can explain route-state changes and boundary/chokepoint behavior, but crossing a camera zone is normal and should not fail validation by itself. Updated `scripts\validate_offline_routes.py` to load `CameraSpaces` from `thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json` and annotate segment failures with `zones=start:... end:... crossed:...`. Sample command `uv run python .\scripts\validate_offline_routes.py objects --max-objects 1 --warn-only` now shows blocked segments with zone diagnostics such as `hallway2`, `living_room`, `living_room3`, `piano_room11`, and `dining_room4`, while preserving the same hard failure categories (`segment_crosses_blocked_cell`, `segment_crosses_blocker`). Next planner fix remains geometry first, but diagnostics should be kept so camera-zone correlations are visible.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex proof passed; coverage sweep harness start fixed and deployed):** User confirmed the Date A Dex entry-detail test worked: opening a Date A Dex entry now speaks the entry detail including the bio from the collectables-focused entry view, so the prior Date A Dex pending proof is complete. User also started the coverage sweep but stopped before completion. The partial artifacts are enough to identify a harness-level trend: `artifacts\navigation\sweep\default\sweep_results.json` recorded `77` completed runtime routes and all `77` ended as `looped`, all on upper-floor long routes with elapsed times around the 8-second loop detector window. Code review showed `SimpleNavCoverageSweep.StepTeleporting()` installed each route with `SimpleNavBridge.BeginRoute(...)` but never enabled `AccessibilityWatcher._isAutoWalking`, so `ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute()` never drove movement. Fix: added `AccessibilityWatcher.TryStartCoverageSweepRoute(...)` and `AccessibilityWatcher.StopCoverageSweepRoute()` so the sweep starts and stops the same route-driven autowalk state used by normal navigation; `SimpleNavCoverageSweep` now uses those methods and reports `input_failed:<reason>` if startup is unavailable. Documented the contract in `docs\game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal build compiled but failed at plugin copy with access denied; elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `EA00E68DF47A05AFE292429E897B81A9FC3EE408B324B1AA62713401263DF002`. Pending proof: restart the game so the new DLL loads, rerun the coverage sweep, and check that the next results are no longer uniformly `looped` at about 8 seconds.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex collectables focus now reads entry detail):** User corrected the symptom: opening a Date A Dex entry focuses the number of collectables, not the Back button; that collectables speech is acceptable, but the entry/bio must also be spoken. RCA from source: `DateADex.OpenEntry(int)` intentionally calls `SelectEntryElement()`, which selects `CollectableButton`; the accessibility branch for `CollectableButton` returned only the collectable count, so even with pending entry data available the focused-control speech could still be just collectables. Fix: when `TryBuildDateADexSelectionAnnouncement(...)` sees `CollectableButton` while the Date A Dex bio pane is visible, it now prefers `TryBuildDateADexDetailAnnouncement(..., _pendingDateADexDetailEntry)` and clears the pending entry request to prevent a duplicate. The detail announcement already includes collectables, so the selected control still reports the collectable count as part of the entry read. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `49E15A6A9313C8D647E344A1D2E9C554A643C9A0C7C0B5777E2B495FE64E6046`. Pending proof: restart the game, open Date A Dex, open a met character entry, and confirm the focused collectables control speaks the entry detail including bio and collectable count.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex opened bio speech isolated fix deployed):** User reported the restored baseline still does not read the bio when opening a Date A Dex character entry and instead only speaks the Back button. RCA from code review: the committed `DateADex.OpenEntry(int)` postfix queued a generic pending detail read but discarded the opened entry id; by the time the pending reader ran, it still depended on UI text extraction and focus could already be on the Back button. Fix kept intentionally narrow: `DateADexPatches.OnOpenEntry(int id)` now resolves `DateADex.Instance.GetEntry(id)` and passes that `DateADexEntry` to `AccessibilityWatcher.RequestDateADexEntryAnnouncement(entry)`. The pending detail builder still reads visible UI text first, but uses the opened entry as fallback for object, bio, voice actor, likes, and dislikes when UI text is not readable yet; unmet entries use localized non-spoiler text. No list focus hook and no keyboard focus changes were added. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `2CE425EBD80B356C8EBF1A8B3FE3753640E5FE93F0759C54EAA02C9C92DEEFEB`. Pending proof: restart the game, open Date A Dex, focus a met character entry, press confirm/open entry, and confirm it speaks the bio instead of only Back.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex opened bio detail now carries entry data and deployed):** User clarified the remaining Date A Dex bug: list focus reads the current dateable name correctly, but opening an entry does not read the bio. RCA from code review: the `OpenEntry(int)` postfix only queued a generic detail read, so the later pending handler still depended on UI/animation-readable fields and private `currentEntry` reflection. That can fail even though `OpenEntry(int)` already has the committed entry index and has just filled the bio fields. Fix: `DateADexPatches.OnOpenEntry(int id)` now resolves `DateADex.Instance.GetEntry(id)` and passes the `DateADexEntry` into `AccessibilityWatcher.RequestDateADexEntryAnnouncement(entry)`. The bio builder uses that entry as the authoritative current entry and falls back to `CharDYK`, `VoActor`, `CharLikes`, and `CharDislikes` if the UI text is not readable yet; unmet entries use a localized non-spoiler unmet description. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `10A9568BC66B22934507DA764557E3CBA14D3BD43165D3E7D04F4132ED8426E3`. Pending proof: restart the game, open Date A Dex, focus a dateable, press confirm/open entry, and confirm the bio detail is spoken.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex list focus speech moved to direct event hook and deployed):** User reported that after the keyboard-arrow correction, Date A Dex entries stopped reading again. RCA from code review: Date A Dex uses `CircularScrollingList` / recycled `DexEntryButton` row objects and can change the focused entry without giving accessibility a reliable new selected `GameObject`. Polling `ControllerMenuUI.GetCurrentSelectedControl()` plus duplicate suppression is therefore fragile for Date A Dex row speech. Fix: `DateADexPatches` now also hooks `DateADex.OnEntryFocused(...)` and passes the focused `DexEntryButton` to `AccessibilityWatcher.RequestDateADexFocusedEntryAnnouncement(...)`. The watcher announces the non-spoiler entry heading only while the Date A Dex list is active, suppresses duplicate row echoes, and leaves the opened-entry `OpenEntry(int)` detail hook intact. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `ABCE812822A235F8332D0A11142B9ADA53D2439D91A9B5645AA3E48CD569508B`. Pending proof: restart the game, open Date A Dex, arrow through the list, and confirm each focused row reads while arrow snapping remains normal.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — keyboard arrow focus fallback corrected and deployed):** User reported that the Date A Dex speech build regressed keyboard arrow handling so focus behaved like mouse cursor movement rather than snapping between options. RCA from code review: the accessibility choice-keyboard helper used `ControllerMenuUI.SetCurrentlySelected(..., isViaPointer: true)` unconditionally. That pointer-style path was originally useful for quick-response buttons, but it changes keyboard focus semantics when used broadly. Fix: `FocusChoice(...)` now attempts ordinary manual selection first (`isViaPointer: false`) so arrow movement keeps controller-style snapping. It retries pointer-style selection only if the target is a `QuickResponseButton` and normal selection did not take. Updated `docs\game-api.md` with the narrower contract. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `CA9600C52FE9C1CA4B44DE12583D57599617BF48A5777DD5061D3EEFBB437869`. Pending proof: restart the game, test keyboard arrows in Date A Dex and dialogue/chat choices, and confirm arrows snap between options while quick responses remain selectable.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — Date A Dex entry speech fixed and deployed):** User reported that Date A Dex entries were not reading properly. RCA from code review: the opened-entry detail announcement was built mostly from visible bio fields (`Item`, `Desc`, `VoActor`, `Likes`, `Dislikes`, `Pronouns`). Different unmet entries share the same visible bio text (`???` plus the unmet description), so duplicate-speech suppression could make entry changes sound like nothing changed; met entries also lacked a stable entry-number heading. Fix: Date A Dex list rows and opened-entry details now build a non-spoiler heading from `DateADexEntry.CharIndex + 1`; opened details read the committed private `DateADex.currentEntry` via reflection. Met entries include character name and object when available; unmet entries announce only entry number plus unknown, so hidden names/objects are not revealed. Added localization keys and documented the contract in `docs\game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `58CAB803F4A27854F3E71F52713257A167A81F048C89A7D549C82612C951D44D`. Pending proof: restart the game, open Date A Dex, move across several met and unmet entries, and confirm each entry announces its entry number and does not go silent on repeated `???` bios.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — post-route facing loop removed and deployed):** User reported that the normal interaction route itself seems fine, but the end behavior loops near arrival. RCA from current code review: after a route ended, `UpdateNavigationState()` still intercepted `_isAutoWalking && !SimpleNavBridge.HasActiveRoute && IsTrackedObjectReached()` and called `TryFaceTrackedObjectAtArrival()`. That helper could return every frame while applying look input until `InteractableManager.activeObject` selected the target; if selection never flipped, navigation never reached `StopNavigationWithAnnouncement("navigation_arrived")`, producing the observed near-end loop. Fix: removed the post-route facing helper and returned the no-active-route proximity path to a clean arrival announcement. Active `SimpleNavRoute` following/completion remains intact. Removed the stale helper methods and updated `docs\game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on plugin DLL access denied, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `4A80AF54F1BD52BA751966478AE752DA617064C567D48583558C732BCBFF30EF`. Pending proof: restart the game, rerun the package/front-door route, and confirm it no longer spins/loops at the end after the route completes.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — root office/hall bake disconnect fixed and deployed):** User correctly challenged the runtime bridge workaround and asked to investigate/fix the disconnect. RCA: `navigable_region.bake.json` had the office side and hallway/package side in separate connected components even though `Doors_Office` is a real traversable door. `scripts\diagnose_navigable_components.py --target 20506 --start-xz 12.20 8.00 --interaction-radius 1.25` on the old bake reported ground `components=36`, start component `25`, and nearest start-to-main/stair gap from `(9.087, 6.961)` to `(10.487, 4.961)` at `2.441m`. Bitmap inspection showed the gap cells were walkable-but-blocked (`X`), not void. Nearby blocker contributors included `SM_Walls_Hall1`, `SM_Doorframe_Small_1`, and `AtticDoor_11_MODEL_UPDATE` under `Doors_Office_Locked`; the current bake only carved a `0.40m` disc at `Doors_*` interactable positions, which created a tiny isolated doorway island rather than connecting office to hallway. Fix: `scripts\bake_navigable_region.py` now also reads authoritative real door entries from `thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json` where `DoorComponent` is present, and carves those at `DOOR_COMPONENT_CARVE_RADIUS=1.50m`; generic name-only `Doors_*` entries keep the old `0.40m` carve. Regenerated `artifacts\navigation\navigable_region.bake.json` and PPMs. Verification: new bake ground components drop to `29`, the failed start is in component `1` with size `43191`, and `uv run python .\scripts\plan_object_route.py --target 20506 --start-xz 12.20 8.00 --start-floor ground --interaction-radius 1.25` now returns `status=ok` with four waypoints `(12.287, 7.961) -> (11.087, 7.361) -> (10.887, 5.161) -> (18.687, -1.439)`. Build verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy failed on the plugin DLL access-denied copy, then elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Deployed DLL hash remains `54330D839F5E0D5DF06DE765E3F1CD26E32D8600154BDB60659B6165DF4949AB`; deployed `navigable_region.bake.json` hash matches bin/artifact hash `E9A11449FCA37EC2D5A29E56CB33B22037300D4EEF7A322A673D5157AE3C6144`. Pending proof: restart the game so the new bake loads, press `Ctrl+F6` on `Check the delivery at the front door.`, and confirm direct package planning now returns `SimpleNavPlanner.Plan ok target=SM_Gift_Lid_MODEL_UPDATE_ORIGIN` instead of falling back to `Doors_Office`/post-door bridge.

**Current session note (2026-05-25 — crash rollback after office portal bridge):** User reported the game crashed when pressing `Ctrl+F6` on `Check the delivery at the front door.`. Log proof shows build `84D6975E...` reached `BeginNavigation`, resolved `Mysterious Box`, logged `SimpleNavPlanner: added door portal bridge edges=1` and `bake loaded`, then stopped before `Plan` returned. Root cause is the new ad hoc `Doors_Office` graph bridge edge causing route planning to hang/crash; user correctly noted SimpleNav should already have door-bridge/traversal handling. Decompiled source confirms the authoritative state: `TutorialController.SetTutorialText(...)` shows `Check the delivery at the front door.` when `TUTORIAL_STATE_2_SAW_THISCORD` is set and `skylar_specs` is still `Unmet`; `UnpauseAndGoToGift()` unlocks office doors, sets the front door moving dateable to `broken`, activates `giftBox`, and refreshes the text. `TutorialTriggerZone.EventType.TriggerDrone` is only for the previous objective and is disabled by `DeliverGiftBox()`; `TutorialThreshold.isDroneThreshold` only calls `GotCloseToGift()` after delivery. `TutorialController.CanInteract(...)` explicitly allows only objects whose `InternalName()` is `skylar` while `skylar_specs` is unmet. Real option set: route/interact with the Skylar Specs gift box (`SM_Gift_MODEL_UPDATE_ORIGIN` or lid, `inkFileName=skylar_specs`, `InternalCharacterName=skylar`), optionally pass through the post-delivery drone threshold to clear drone hover, or repair traversal from office to hallway/front-hall using graph/door transition logic instead of selecting arbitrary hallway objects. Root cause: for `TargetGameObjectId == 0`, object-style arrival semantics were still using planner goal-disc cells and `WaypointArrivalRadius`; those tolerances stack and can finish several meters before the true trigger/front-hall coordinate. **Current session note (2026-05-24 — gift spawn source corrected):** User clarified that the objective still must route somewhere that can cause the gift box to spawn, not merely fail cleanly. Source review found the critical distinction: `TutorialTriggerZone.OnTriggerEnter(...)` with `EventType.TriggerDrone` calls `TutorialController.DeliverGiftBox()`, which starts the delivery sequence; `TutorialThreshold.OnTriggerEnter(...)` with `isDroneThreshold == true` only calls `TutorialController.GotCloseToGift()`, which is post-delivery drone behavior and does not spawn the gift box. Static artifacts show the routeable office-side trigger volume as `===SCENE===/House/Office/TutorialDroneThreshhold_3`, approximate XZ bounds `x=5.90..9.70`, `z=7.70..9.80`; the hall-side `TutorialDroneThreshold` is not the spawn source and can be unrouteable from the office-side nav component. Fix: removed `TutorialThreshold.isDroneThreshold` from the delivery-spawn fallback path. `TutorialObjectiveKind.FrontDoor` now only falls back to an active `TutorialTriggerZone.EventType.TriggerDrone` when the gift box is not pathable, and logs a missing active trigger zone separately. The trigger target radius now uses the collider's horizontal extent instead of half that extent, matching trigger-volume routing semantics. Updated `docs\game-api.md` with the source-level contract. **Current session note (2026-05-24 — proximity arrival now faces object):** User reported that selecting an object already within range immediately announced `Arrived at target` and autowalk did nothing, because the legacy non-route arrival check ignored camera rotation. **Current session note (2026-05-24 — Ctrl+F6 Roomers fallback objective):** User clarified that when there is no tutorial objective, `Ctrl+F6` should derive the next objective from the currently selected Roomers entry, not the DataDex. Implemented a Roomers-only fallback in objective resolution: if tutorial objective kind is `None`, the resolver first reads the focused `RoomersEntryButton.roomersEntry`; if focus is not on a row, it reflects Roomers private `currentEntry` plus `RoomersEntries` to get the current row. It then maps `roomersEntry.character` through character/display/object names to the best active dateable `InteractableObj`, canonicalizes it through existing navigable-interactable resolution, and starts the normal route planner/tracker path. Also fixed `StartNavigationToCurrentTarget()` to honor existing world-target objectives before interactable objectives, matching the office gift-delivery trigger path. Documented the Roomers fields and fallback contract in `docs\game-api.md`. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 match `21CF6F1B2ED08D26F03C20D5F58567B7894A3B417200B08A24DCAD4ACC01C579`. Pending proof: restart the game so this DLL loads, open Roomers with no active tutorial objective, select an active clue, press `Ctrl+F6`, and confirm it routes to that Roomers character/object.

**Follow-up in same 2026-05-24 session:** User reported Roomers clues were not reading on selection. Root cause from decompiled code: `RoomersButton.OnSelect()` calls `Roomers.switchEntryIndex(index)` without `andDisplayInfo`, and that path does not call `RoomersInfo.SetUpPanel(...)`; the current detail panel can therefore be stale when focus moves to a new Roomers row. Fix: selection announcements now detect focused `RoomersEntryButton` rows and build the clue announcement directly from `RoomersEntryButton.roomersEntry` (`questName`, character, object/location, description, and found tips) instead of reading the visible panel. Documented this Roomers focus contract in `docs\game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors; elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded. Pending proof: restart the game, open Roomers, move between clue rows, and confirm each selected clue is spoken immediately.

**Current session note (2026-05-24 — traversal-door picker and tutorial gift delivery trigger):** User clarified that the object picker should include only doors the player can move through, not cabinet-style objects with `door` in the name. Fix: picker door eligibility now requires a real `Door` or `SlidingDoor` component on the interactable, parent, or child; string-name door matching was removed. User also clarified the `Leave the office and reflect/contemplate on your life choices` prompt should trigger the gift-box cutscene rather than target the office door. Decompiled root cause: `TutorialController.DeliverGiftBox0()` enables `TutorialTriggerZone.EventType.TriggerDrone`; entering that trigger calls `DeliverGiftBox()`, and `UnpauseAndGoToGift()` later activates `giftBox`. Fix: the office-exit tutorial objective now routes to the active drone trigger zone's world coordinate via reflected `TutorialController.triggerZones`, labeled `Gift delivery trigger`, instead of the previous hall-side office-door coordinate. The `Check the delivery at the front door` objective still prefers the active `giftBox`, but now falls back to `frontDoor` if the gift box cannot resolve to a navigable zone. Documented these findings in `docs\game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` passed with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Normal deploy initially failed because `D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\Common\Date Everything\BepInEx\plugins\DateEverythingAccess.dll` was denied/locked; user then approved elevated deploy, and `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded, copying `DateEverythingAccess.dll` to the BepInEx plugins folder. **Current session note (2026-05-24 — narrowed object picker and office computer target):** User retested a new game and reported Ctrl+Shift+F6 started with `129` objects, which was too many; they chose the narrower rule of doors plus dated objects. Root cause was the previous use of Unity `Renderer.isVisible`, which is camera/global visibility and not an appropriate object-list scope. Fix: known-object picker now includes only door interactables plus dateable objects whose save relationship status is no longer `Unmet`; the visible-object rule was removed from the picker. User also reported `No path found to monitor` from inside the office. Static interactable export showed multiple `mac_computer` siblings, including `Monitor` entries at approximately `(0,0,0)` outside the office, while the reachable PC/keyboard/mouse entries are at office desk world coordinates. **Current session note (2026-05-23 — blocker classification audit for fireplace/furniture):** Investigated the user concern that the fireplace was misclassified as a wall and whether other furniture hit the same path. Added reproducible audit helper `scripts/audit_blocker_classification.py` and documented the result in `docs/game-api.md`. Current blocker export has `1014` mesh records: `744` navigation blockers, `103` `FootprintAreaExceedsMax`, `86` `ThinFrameAABBSuppressed`, and `81` `TinyFootprint`. The bake uses `68` wall-segment records: `15` real wall records, `31` door/frame records, `19` exterior fence records, `1` thin art frame, and `2` furniture-name matches. The two furniture candidates are `SM_Fireplace` at `===SCENE===/House/LivingRoom/Fireplace` (`FootprintAreaExceedsMax`, `IsWallLikeFatVictim=true`, 70 used wall segments, no descendant nav blocker) and `Bookshelf_MODEL_UPDATE` under office bookshelf (`ThinFrameAABBSuppressed`, 46 used wall segments). Separate but related risk: large furniture meshes rejected outright by area cap include Dining Table and Kitchen Counters with no descendant navigation blocker, while Sofa, Desk, Piano Body, and Bed have some descendant blockers. No runtime/build change made yet. Next likely fix should split structural wall/doorframe segment rasterization from furniture obstacle rasterization instead of relying on the current geometry-only wall-like victim heuristic.

**Current session note (2026-05-23 — game-side furniture vs wall check):** Checked decompiled game code, `TagManager.asset`, and exact AssetRipper scene YAML blocks for `Fireplace`, `Table_Dining`, `SM_Kitchen_Counters_separated_MODEL_UPDATE`, `SM_Walls_Living`, and `Bookshelf_MODEL_UPDATE`. The game does not provide a reliable physics category for wall vs furniture: these all sit on layer 0 (`Default`), tag `Untagged`, static flags 0, active 1, with non-trigger MeshCollider-style physical collision. `BetterPlayerControl` moves by Rigidbody velocity and ordinary Unity collisions; walls and furniture are both physical obstacles. Interaction selection is separate: a camera raycast through `~dateviatorIgnores` looks for `InteractableObj`, and `InteractableObj.Initialize()` forces interactable objects back to layer 0. Some special objects toggle colliders at runtime (`Dresser`, `DresserWall`, doors), but scripts are not enough to classify all furniture because Kitchen Counters look structurally similar to `SM_Walls_Living` at the component level. Conclusion: exporter classification should not expect game layer/tag help; use hierarchy/name/path as the structural-vs-furniture signal, with runtime overrides for special scripted objects.

**Current session note (2026-05-23 — living-room fireplace smoothing fix):** User retested the semantic waypoint build and reported doors now work; route almost reaches the Magnifying Glass but gets stuck in the living room. Fresh `LogOutput.log` proves the door blocker is cleared: the route advances through repeated `DoorApproach` / `DoorOpening` / `DoorExit` waypoints for `Doors_Bedroom`, descends to ground floor, then times out at player `(-0.18, -0.60, -25.69)` while trying to reach `wp[25]=(-3.01, -0.50, -25.74)`. Runtime probe reports `runtimeBlocker=chest=<clear> ankle=Fireplace layer=0 dist=0.44`. Root cause: `SimpleNavPlanner` smoothing collapsed a safe route into a long leg from `wp[24]=(17.39, -0.50, -7.14)` to `wp[25]=(-3.01, -0.50, -25.74)` that skimmed the fireplace. The fireplace exists in `thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json`, but its full footprint is huge and rejected as `FootprintAreaExceedsMax`; the bake represents it with wall-like slice segments, so the old sparse Bresenham `SegmentIsClear` could miss grazed cells. Fix: `SimpleNavPlanner.SegmentIsClear(...)` now samples candidate smoothing shortcuts at half-cell spacing and rejects any touched non-navigable cell. This only tightens waypoint smoothing; it does not change A* graph connectivity or door semantics. **Current session note (2026-05-22 — fixed premature large-radius route arrival):** After diagnostic build `56BA07A...`, user reran and no `SimpleNav route frame` lines appeared. Log showed routes planned and auto-walk started, then the user stopped them; absence of frame diagnostics proved `ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute()` was returning before the movement-command section. Root cause: `SimpleNavBridge.HasArrivedAtRouteTarget()` checked only XZ distance to `route.TargetPosition` against the target's full interaction radius. Large-radius targets (`InteractionRadius=7.5`) can be within XZ radius at route start, especially when the player is vertically above/below the target or near a closet door, so the route entered the final camera-selection hold immediately and never walked to the first/final waypoint. Fix: `HasArrivedAtRouteTarget()` now returns true only after the active waypoint index is the final waypoint and the player is within the normal waypoint arrival radius of that final waypoint, then it applies the target interaction-radius check and first-person selection gate. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` succeeds with `0` warnings and `0` errors; elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeds. **Current session note (2026-05-22 — route plans installed but runtime completion failed):** User reported that routes now plan, but auto-walk does not reliably walk to or complete them. Runtime log review showed `SimpleNavPlanner.Plan ok` and `SimpleNavBridge.BeginRoute` are firing, so the failure is not route installation. Evidence split into final-pose/selection failures: routes such as `Bed_MODEL_UPDATE` advance through waypoints and then sit inside the large `7.5m` interaction disc without satisfying `InteractableManager.activeObject == target && IsPlayerInRange`; other object targets like the magnifying glass route also show the planner accepting broad-radius endpoint cells. Root cause found in `SimpleNavPlanner.ResolveTargetCollider(...)`: it only checked `io.GetComponent<Collider>()` on the exact `InteractableObj` GameObject, missing colliders that live on child or parent objects. When collider resolution failed, the planner skipped the near-collider goal band and used the full interaction-radius disc, which can end at a reachable but not first-person-selectable pose. Fix: `ResolveTargetCollider(...)` now selects the best enabled non-trigger collider from the target object, children, then parents by closest bounds point to the interactable anchor. Additional arrival hardening in `AccessibilityWatcher.ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute()`: if the route is within radius but the game has not selected the target, it always holds/turns the camera (small yaw scan when nominal look input is zero) instead of falling through to `navigation_arrived`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` succeeds with `0` warnings and `0` errors; elevated `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeds after normal deploy hit plugin DLL access denied. Local and deployed DLL SHA-256 both match `D81E861B683C7E54E30276CF50FB8388161FD3770513FE0608125DCA5B3F57B7`. Pending proof: restart the game and retest the same routes, especially Bed and Magnifying Glass. Check the new waypoint logs: final waypoints for ordinary object targets should be near the resolved collider band, not merely anywhere inside the `7.5m` disc. If they still stall, next instrumentation should log the active object name/range state and camera raycast blocker while waiting at final route arrival.

**Design clarification (2026-05-22 — unified interaction readiness):** User clarified the target architecture: avoid a separate door interaction system where possible. Treat doors as normal interactable objects at the input layer, then add door-specific effect predicates only where the game does. Current best model: shared base predicate for all routed interactables is `BetterPlayerControl`/camera targeting reflected by `InteractableManager.activeObject == chosenInteractable && IsPlayerInRange`; alternate/world-effect interactables add `Interactable.CheckCanUse()`; door interactables add the door-effect layer (`moving`, `blockInteraction`, `locked`, `collidedWithPlayer`, swing completion). Route planning should use the same layering: object target pose first, then for pass-through/target doors compute a stand waypoint that can satisfy the door effect layer without colliding, while runtime verifies the live door fields.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — door interaction pose in route planning):** Current computed routes only partially account for satisfying door interaction. If the selected target is itself a `Door`, `SimpleNavPlanner` filters goal cells to a hinge-distance band (`DoorHingeBandInnerM=1.5`, `DoorHingeBandOuterM=3.0`) so the route terminates outside the likely swing arc. If a route passes through a door en route, the planner only tags the nearby route segment with the door name (`DoorTagRadiusM=2.5`); it does not insert an explicit stand-here-before-interact waypoint. Runtime `SimpleNavBridge.TryOpenDoorIfNeeded(...)` then decides when to call `door.Interact()` based on range and some live door fields. This means route planning can and should do more: derive static door interaction stand candidates from door transform/hinge, `DoorOpenType`, swing `range`, portal/opening metadata when available, and player radius, then force the route through a candidate outside the closed/open swing envelope before crossing the doorway. Runtime must still verify live-only fields (`moving`, `blockInteraction`, `locked`, `collidedWithPlayer`) because those cannot be known offline.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — door block check is runtime-only):** Door player-blocking is not in `Interactable.CheckCanUse()` and is not an offline route-planning predicate. In `Door.cs`, `OpenDoor(...)` / `CloseDoor(...)` only proceed when `!blockInteraction && !collidedWithPlayer`, `Interact()` returns early while private `moving`, locked doors only play a sound, and the swing coroutine stops short when `collidedWithPlayer && stopOnCollision`; `collidedWithPlayer` is set by `OnCollisionEnter` / `OnCollisionExit`. `SlidingDoor.cs` has the same shape with private `moving`, `stateLock`, `locked`, `collidedWithPlayer`, and `stopOnCollision`. These checks are cheaper and more reliable than geometric prediction for runtime execution because they are the game-owned state, but they cannot replace route planning because they only exist after the live player/door collision state is known. Best split: offline planner should route to a conservative approach point outside the swing volume; runtime executor should read the door fields, interact only when `CheckCanUse()` / node-lock, range, not moving, not blocked, not collided, and not locked are satisfied, then verify `open`/rotation changed.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — `Interactable.CheckCanUse` traced):** `Interactable.CheckCanUse()` is not the universal interaction-success boolean. It only checks `noderequired()` / Ink visit state plus `!stateLock`. Direct game call sites found: `GameController.SelectObj(...)` before invoking `AlternateInteractions`, and `InteractableManager.UpdatePlayerTarget(...)` for alternate prompt display. Normal alternate interaction flow is therefore camera raycast/range -> `InteractableManager.Interact(...)` -> `GameController.SelectObj(...)` -> `alternateInteraction.CheckCanUse()` -> `alternateInteraction.Interact()` -> `ToggleInteractedWith(...)`. Door effect success is still below this: `Door.Interact()` can return early if private `moving`, can play a locked sound without opening if `locked`, and `OpenDoor` / `CloseDoor` only change state when `!blockInteraction && !collidedWithPlayer`; the swing coroutine can also stop short when `collidedWithPlayer && stopOnCollision`. Sliding doors similarly gate on private `moving`, `stateLock`, `locked`, and collision/stop-on-collision during movement. Practical implication: for ordinary object-route arrival, the game-owned input-targeting condition remains `activeObject == chosenInteractable && IsPlayerInRange`. For route-changing interactables, add `CheckCanUse()` to preflight when going through the normal alternate-interaction model, but still observe or reflect per-object state to know whether the route-changing effect actually happened.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — interaction conditions reframed):** Reframed the interaction question as layered success conditions. Layer 1 is input targeting in `BetterPlayerControl.Update()`: player state must be `CanControl`, camera raycast must hit the chosen object's own `InteractableObj` transform, and camera-to-closest-collider distance must be inside `InteractionRadius`. This is the ordinary-object navigation responsibility. Layer 2 is `InteractableManager.Interact(...)`: tutorial permission unless forced, not `TALKING`, active object non-null, and `inrange`. Layer 3 is `GameController.CanSelectObj(...)` / `SelectObj(...)`: Dateviators/glasses, charge, talked-today, realization, and dialogue setup; navigation should not own these for ordinary object routes. Layer 4 is the object's own effect: alternate interactions require `Interactable.CheckCanUse()` (`noderequired()` empty or visited, and not `stateLock`). Navigation only needs this layer for route-changing objects such as doors and teleporters. Practical next fix remains: ordinary tracked-object arrival should wait for `activeObject == chosenInteractable && IsPlayerInRange`, then stop; route doors/teleporters keep their special state gates because traversal depends on the world state actually changing.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — interaction boolean narrowed):** Investigated `GameController.CanSelectObj(...)` as a candidate navigation readiness boolean. It is not the right gate for object-route arrival because it has no player position/facing/raycast/range checks and includes rules navigation should not own: Dateviators/glasses state, charges, talked-today, realization, and dialogue eligibility. The game's actual in-world interaction readiness is produced in `BetterPlayerControl.Update()` as private local `flag3` after the camera raycast hits an `InteractableObj` and the camera-to-closest-collider distance is inside `InteractionRadius`; the accessible mirror is `Singleton<InteractableManager>.Instance.activeObject == chosenInteractable && Singleton<InteractableManager>.Instance.IsPlayerInRange`. `InteractableManager.UIon` is also not suitable because it is prompt/highlight presentation state affected by equipped/date/alternate interaction rules. Right implementation direction: route arrival for ordinary tracked objects should wait for this active-object + in-range expression, using camera/body steering until it becomes true. Doors remain the explicit exception: segment door handling can continue using `Door.Interact()` plus door state (`open`, `moving`, collision) because navigation must actually open/close the door for traversal.

**Current session note (2026-05-22 — stairs pipeline fixed, upper component split remains):** Implemented the stairs fix in `scripts\bake_navigable_region.py`: after writing `artifacts\navigation\navigable_region.bake.json`, the bake now runs `scripts\derive_inter_floor_edges.py` as a required post-pass and fails if a multi-floor bake still has no `inter_floor_edges`. Regenerated the bake; it now contains one `stair_ramp` edge from `===SCENE===/House/Hallway/Stairs` and one `CrawlspaceLadder` teleporter. Verification: `uv run python .\scripts\bake_navigable_region.py` appended both edge types, a synthetic A* route from the default ground start to the upper stair endpoint succeeds with edge kinds `stairs` + `walk`, and `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` succeeded with `0` warnings and `0` errors. Important caveat: the saved `upper_hallway_light` plan still returns `no_path`, but it is no longer a missing-stairs issue. The upper stair endpoint is navigable and connected to the ground start; the target goal cells are in a different upper-floor connected component about `1.4m` away at the nearest boundary. Next routing fix should investigate upper-floor connected-component / target-goal segmentation rather than adding another stair system. Interaction investigation correction: `GameController.viewState != TALKING` is only a final `Interact()` guard, not the meaningful can-initiate predicate. The real readiness path is `BetterPlayerControl` camera raycast + range -> `HandleChargingUpCompleted()` -> `InteractableManager.Interact()` -> `GameController.CanSelectObj` / `SelectObj` for charge, talked-today, realized, and dialogue-start rules.

**Investigation update (2026-05-22 — two object-first blockers identified before the stairs fix):** Do not start coverage sweeps yet. Two root-cause tracks are active. (1) Interaction readiness is game-camera-raycast driven, not proximity-only. Base game path: `BetterPlayerControl.Update()` raycasts from `_camera.transform.position - _camera.transform.forward * 0.25f` along `_camera.transform.forward`; hit transform must directly have `InteractableObj`; range is `Distance(hitInfo.collider.ClosestPointOnBounds(cameraPos), cameraPos) < InteractionRadius`; then it publishes `InteractableManager.activeObject` and `IsPlayerInRange`. `InteractableManager.Interact()` consumes only when active object + in-range + not talking. Current mod mismatch: `ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute()` treats route proximity plus flat yaw-facing `route.TargetPosition` as arrival, writes only `look.x`, and `UpdateNavigationState()` can stop via `IsTrackedObjectReached()` before the route branch proves the game has selected the target. Next work should react to `InteractableManager.activeObject == trackedInteractable && IsPlayerInRange`, using `BetterPlayerControl.look` yaw/pitch only until the base game selects the object. (2) Multi-floor routes failed because the deployed/committed bake lacked `inter_floor_edges`; the follow-up note above records the pipeline fix and the remaining upper connected-component issue. Findings also documented in `docs/game-api.md`.

**Recontextualization update (2026-05-22 — object-first navigation is current):** Claude's redesign supersedes the older room-to-room / transition-sweep model. Current navigation is object-first: user selects/tracks an interactable, `SimpleNavPlanner` plans directly from the live player position to the target object's reachable interaction band over `artifacts\navigation\navigable_region.bake.json`, and `SimpleNavBridge`/`AccessibilityWatcher.ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute` follow the resulting waypoint polyline. `CameraSpaces` are now treated as camera/runtime labels only, not walkable geometry or routing topology. The old generated room graph, local maps, transition imports, door-contract scripts, and split door/open-passage sweep tooling were removed in O7/O8 cleanup commits. The only active in-game navigation sweep harness is `SimpleNavCoverageSweep` on `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F8`, reading `artifacts\navigation\sweep\default\sweep_manifest.json` and route JSONs, then validating object-route coverage by teleporting, autowalking, stamping verified cells, and recording flat outcomes (`arrived`, `looped`, `stalled`, `door_failed`, `budget`, `no_path`, etc.). Current code surfaces to inspect first: `SimpleNavPlanner.cs`, `SimpleNavRoute.cs`, `SimpleNavBridge.cs`, `SimpleNavCoverageSweep.cs`, `ObjectTracker.cs`, `AccessibilityWatcher.cs` route branch around `ApplyAutoWalkSimpleRoute`, plus scripts `bake_navigable_region.py`, `derive_inter_floor_edges.py`, `plan_object_route.py`, and `sweep_coverage_planner.py`. Current data note: the checked-in sweep manifest has 6,677 entries (6,541 planned ok, 136 no_path) but the checked-in `sweep_results.json` only has two `looped` rows, so runtime coverage proof is still pending/incomplete. Also verify regeneration order before relying on old route catalogues: the current committed bake has no `inter_floor_edges` object while existing sweep routes can still mention `stairs`, indicating the sweep catalogue may be stale relative to the latest bake. No code changes in this recontextualization pass.

Included changes: `Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1` now uses floor-middle mesh slice planes `(0.5, 13.5)`; `bake_navigable_region.py` filters walkable slabs by vertical extent, rasterizes wall segments, applies Euclidean dilation, and carves 0.4m door discs from `thirdpersongreybox-interactables.json`; regenerated `thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json`, `navigable_region.bake.json`, and ground/upper PPM debug maps are included. No C# changes; no mod rebuild required. Known follow-up remains navigation validation around doorframe/window/archway blockers and route connectivity.

**Current session note (2026-05-22 — wall-FAT rasterization shipped; doorframe-AABB gap exposed):** Wall-mesh-FAT bake-blocker fix landed end-to-end. `Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1` `Get-MeshColliderRecord` now emits `Footprint.Segments` (world-space 2D line segments from triangle/slice-plane intersections, tagged with source `PlaneY`) alongside the existing convex-hull footprint; rejected meshes get `Footprint.RejectionReason` and `Footprint.IsWallLikeFatVictim` flags. Victim gate: vertical extent in [3.0, 22.0] m AND `TopY` in [12.0, 13.5] (ground ceiling) or [25.0, 33.0] (upper ceiling / attic roof) AND at least one segment — admits all 11 combined-walls (`SM_Walls_Living/Hall1/Kitchen/Dining/Laundry/Office/Closet_Office` ground; `SM_Walls_Bedroom/Hall2/Gym/Attic` upper) plus Fireplace; excludes Monitor/Body/Sofa/Bed/HVAC/trees/yacht (these were leaking through earlier-iteration gates and carving room interiors). Bands are scene-specific per [[user-mod-reuse-goal]] and need re-derivation for other scenes. `bake_navigable_region.py` adds a supercover `_rasterize_segment` and a wall-mesh pass that filters segments by per-floor Y-band (PlaneY) and rasterizes into `blocked_bm` before dilation. Dilation switched from Chebyshev box to Euclidean disc — minor gain. Counts: ground 8 wall meshes / 1362 segments rasterized, upper 4 wall meshes / 1000 segments. Ground navigable 109852→68181 cells; upper 94799→55608 cells. Spot-check `SM_Walls_Bedroom` segments at plane 13.1 correctly trace the bedroom walls including a 1.13m doorway gap at Z=-5.72 to -6.85. Player capsule r=0.4m verified authoritative at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\extracted\Ripped\ExportedProject\Assets\GameObject\Player.prefab:407`. **Defect surfaced — separate fresh-session task:** Python replan of all three saved routes (bedroom_closet_box, dining_table, upper_hallway_light) returns `status=no_path`. Connected-components: ground has 51 components (largest 38511 cells), upper 36 components — rooms traversable individually, room-to-room sealed. Root cause spot-checked at bedroom door `(-10.724, 12.82, -6.462)`: `SM_Doorframe_Small_7` is a **separate MeshCollider** admitted as a regular NavigationBlocker with AABB `x=[-14.79, -10.33] z=[-6.96, -5.64]` (4.46×1.32m) engulfing the door swing. It existed in the OLD bake too — the OLD routes appeared to succeed only because the bedroom walls were missing and A* routed *around* the doorframe AABB *through* wall geometry. Two errors cancelled. Fresh-session task = extend the slice-and-rasterize technique to doorframe meshes (`SM_Doorframe_*`, possibly `Windows_*`, archways); audit all `NavigationBlockers` whose AABB exceeds door-sized openings first, then decide gate per bucket. Probably reuses `IsWallLikeFatVictim` machinery with a relaxed vertical-extent threshold (doorframes are 2.0-2.4m tall, below current `WallMinVerticalExtent=3.0`). See [[project-navigation-bake-doorframe-gap]]. Saved route artifacts (`route.*.json`) are OLD bake outputs and NOT a valid oracle. **Build/deploy state:** no C# changes this session; no rebuild needed. Repo dirty files: `scripts/Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1`, `scripts/bake_navigable_region.py`, `artifacts/navigation/navigable_region.bake.json`, `artifacts/navigation/navigable_region.{ground,upper}.ppm`, `artifacts/navigation/thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json` (1139 NavigationBlockers / 1026 MeshColliders with new Segment/RejectionReason/IsWallLikeFatVictim fields). No commit yet — pending doorframe work since the bake is currently mid-design.

**Phase:** Navigation redesign — tasks C and B complete; A in progress (asset extraction done, mesh parser written and verified, slicing/wire-up pending)
**Currently working on:** Task A mesh-asset parser is written and validated. New helper script `scripts\Read-UnityMeshAsset.ps1` exports `Read-UnityMeshAsset` which loads an AssetRipper-exported `!u!43 Mesh` `.asset` file and returns vertices, triangles (all submeshes), stride, and the authored `m_LocalAABB`. Self-verified against `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\extracted\Ripped\ExportedProject\Assets\Mesh\SM_Light_Recessed_1.asset`: returns 170 vertices, 110 triangles (= (42+96+192)/3 across the asset's 3 submeshes), stride 48; computed AABB center/extent match the authored `m_LocalAABB` to 7 decimal places; 0 out-of-range triangle indices. Next sub-steps (start in a fresh session — current context is large): (1) extend `scripts\Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1` to point at the Premium scene and add a `$MeshDirectory` param defaulting to the Premium `Assets\Mesh\`; (2) build a one-time guid→`.asset` path index by scanning `Mesh\*.asset.meta` for the `guid:` line; (3) add a section-type-64 (`MeshCollider`) parser capturing `m_GameObject`, `m_Enabled`, `m_IsTrigger`, `m_Convex`, and `m_Mesh.guid`; (4) add a chest-height triangle slicer using the existing `MinimumBlockingTopY=0.35` / `MaximumBlockingBottomY=2.35` band — with a mesh-AABB band prefilter first, then per-triangle slice that emits a `{ Kind: "TriangleSoup", Triangles: [[{x,z},{x,z},{x,z}], ...] }` footprint for only the band-intersecting triangles (decision rationale: AABB/convex-hull footprints reintroduce the exact L-shape negative-space problem that motivated slicing in the first place); (5) merge the mesh records into the existing `PrimitiveColliders` and `NavigationBlockers` arrays with `ColliderType="MeshCollider"`, flip `Filtering.MeshCollidersUnsupported` to `false`. Verification target: regenerate `artifacts\navigation\thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json` and confirm `NavigationBlockers` count grows from 67 with sane mesh footprints (currently 2,317 MeshColliders skipped). User pre-approved the design before writing the parser; no further design questions needed for the wire-up.

**Task A asset-extraction (2026-05-16):** Discovered the original Free AssetRipper export at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12\extracted\Ripped` writes every `m_Mesh:` reference as the stub guid `0000000deadbeef15deadf00d0000000` (4,689 of 4,689 MeshCollider refs + MeshFilter refs combined). Free build is hard-locked to this behavior — no settings toggle exposes mesh geometry. Purchased AssetRipper Premium (`AssetRipper.GUI.Premium.exe`) at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\extracted\` and drove it headlessly via its HTTP API on port 17894 (`--headless --port N`). Key settings to unlock mesh export: `BundledAssetsExportMode=GroupByAssetType` (NOT `DirectExport`, which leaves bundles as opaque `.bundle` files), `EnableAssetDeduplication=true` (Premium-only — name-collision-aware dedup with a Meshes column), `EnableStaticMeshSeparation=true` (Premium-only; finds 0 hits in this game because nothing is statically batched, but harmless). To avoid OOM during export on a 16 GB box, also set `AudioExportFormat=Yaml` (Free run hit `OutOfMemoryException` in `AudioClipDecoder.TryDecode` with `Default`), `ScriptContentLevel=Level0`, `ScriptExportMode=DllExportWithoutRenaming`. Run sequence: launch headless → POST `/Settings/Update` with the above → POST `/LoadFolder` with the game path (this BLOCKS through the Processing pipeline; ~15 min wall clock) → POST `/Export/UnityProject` with the output path. The export crashed the host OS at ~58% (system-wide OOM, not server-side) BUT the partial export at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\extracted\Ripped` already contained everything Task A needs: the full `ThirdPersonGreybox.unity` (46.9 MB, written early in the run) and 3,731 mesh `.asset` files in `ExportedProject/Assets/Mesh/`. Resolution check: scene `m_Mesh:` refs now show 0 deadbeef stubs (vs. 4,689 in Free), with varied real guids that map via `.meta` files to named mesh assets (`SM_Light_Recessed_1`, `SM_Fence_1`, `SM_Tree_Big_2`, etc.). PowerShell parser validated end-to-end on `SM_Light_Recessed_1.asset`: `m_IndexBuffer` is plain hex little-endian UInt16 (when `m_IndexFormat: 0`), `_typelessdata` is plain hex with `stride = m_DataSize / m_VertexCount` (48 bytes/vertex here) and position as the first 12 bytes (float32 xyz, channel-0 metadata confirms `offset: 0, format: 0, dimension: 3`). Decoded AABB matches the documented `localAABB` to 6 decimal places. **Do NOT rerun the export** — the partial output is sufficient. **Do NOT touch the Free export at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12\extracted\Ripped`** — `Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1` still points there and the navigation graph data lives there.

**Currently working on (continued):** Task C of the SimpleNav handoff stack is done. `Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1` now emits an `OcclusionPortals` array (16 entries, all resolved to their parent Door by GameObjectId), and `SimpleNav.TryResolveDoorApproach` consults that data: when a portal is available for the door, the lateral component of the approach point is snapped to the portal center on the axis perpendicular to (door → destination), so the player aligns with the doorway opening instead of sidestepping out of a heuristic swing arc. Falls back to the original 0.5m lateral-sign heuristic for doors without portal data.

**Current session note (2026-05-19 — residuals work paused pending unified-model refactor):** Imported fresh sweeps against the deployed build. Door: 8 fail / 20 (same 8 routes as the 2026-05-18 baseline — wall-snap landed but didn't move the needle on the remaining D1 cases). OpenPassage: 15 fail / 32 (down from 17; the Class T1 dorian-overlap cases are gone, confirming the dorian filter cleared them). Override file did NOT mutate on import — Step A's `-WriteOverrides:$false` default is holding. Retried Step C (door `$clearDistance` 0.8→1.2 in `Build-NavigationGraph.ps1:941`); no effect on the 4 wall-clip door failures, reverted to 0.8. Confirmed Step C `$clearDistance` only consumes in the `"Door"` branch — OpenPassage uses authored `FromCrossingAnchor`/`ToCrossingAnchor` directly, so a Door-only knob was never going to address T2 wall failures. Inspected the two office-door failures (`office→hallway`, `office→office_closet`) raw-JSON: different symptoms (`door-threshold-advance-local` reached vs. `localContext=None`) but same domain — the door FSM isn't engaging or completing reliably for office doors. The codebase's door FSM has accumulated ~3000 lines of phase-specific handling across `AccessibilityWatcher.DoorNavigation.cs`, including room-specific hacks like `IsFocusedBathroom1NoSourceBridgeStep`. **User raised a structural point that supersedes the residuals plan:** every connection is conceptually a passage; doors are passages with an open/closed precondition gate. The Door vs OpenPassage code split (separate FSM phases, separate routing geometry, visibility-based A* filtering) is historical baggage, not a correct design. Visibility is the wrong traversability metric (you can see places you can't walk, and vice versa); the right question is whether autowalk locomotion can cross the boundary. **Conclusion: residuals plan is parked.** Steps A (override switch) and B (T1 reclassifications absorbed via dorian filter) aligned with the target model and remain. Steps C/D/F are tactical-only and live in code we want to retire — not worth deeper investment. The remaining 8 door + 15 passage failures persist as known and acceptable for now; the productive next step is scoping a unified-passage-with-optional-precondition refactor, not another round of geometric tuning. **Memory:** new project memory `project-navigation-unified-model.md` captures the target architecture and supersedes `project-navigation-sweep-split.md`. Source-of-truth plan file at `C:\Users\amock\.claude\plans\navigation-broad-residuals.md` is now historical reference — do not extend it. **Next session:** start a clean scoping conversation for the unified-passage refactor; do not resume residuals geometry work.

**Current session note (2026-05-18 — wall-snap + auto-recovery removal + bogus-pair excision):** Three changes landed and deployed (DLL + graph JSON), pending in-game sweep validation against the 8 door / 17 open-passage baseline imported at the start of session. (1) **Wall-snap pass in `scripts\Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`.** New `$BlockersPath` param + `Initialize-WallAabbCache` + `Find-NearbyWallAabb` + `Get-WallSnappedPoint` helpers (~80 lines added near the top). Loads `MeshColliders` filtered to `SM_Walls_*` (NOT `NavigationBlockers` — the thin-wall filter excludes most named walls). Called on door `sourceClearPoint` / `destinationClearPoint` after `Snap-FloorY` runs. Snap params: 0.6m XZ clearance, 1.5m max perpendicular shift, 0.2m step. Walks both perpendicular signs, picks the smaller shift that resolves the conflict. **Important context for next session:** the plan's first attempt (bumping `clearDistance` from 0.8 to 1.2) was reverted — it only affects the `"Door"` case in `Get-ConnectorGeometry`, NOT `OpenPassage`, so it never could have addressed T2 wall-clipping. The snap pass replaces that approach with a generic post-hoc XZ shift. Pre-sweep diff vs. deployed graph: `bathroom1->hallway` source shifted Z by -0.30, `hallway->bathroom1` source/dest each shifted Z by ±0.40, `gym->gym_closet` destination shifted Z by -0.60. Other D1 cases (`upper_hallway↔attic`, `gym_closet->gym`, `office->*`) had zero shift because their walls (`SM_Walls_Gym`, `SM_Walls_Hall2`) aren't in the export at all, or because the blocker is a door GameObject, not a wall. Expected: door failures 7 → ~5 (clears `bathroom1↔hallway`; possibly `gym->gym_closet`); the rest unchanged for known reasons. Snap currently scoped to Doors only; OpenPassage T2 extension deferred until Doors signal validates the lever. (2) **Removed door auto-recovery in `SimpleNavBridge.TryOpenActiveDoorIfNeeded`.** Per user complaint: a real player wouldn't see doors re-trigger multiple times — they'd step back and wait. Deleted the stuck-recovery branch that fired `ForceDoorClosed` + re-Interact up to 3 times. Replaced with a clean `skipReason = "door-stuck-player-blocking"` that lets the step time out honestly. Also removed dead state: `_doorStuckRecoveryStepKey`, `_doorStuckRecoveryAttempts`, `DoorStuckRecoveryAttemptsPerStep`, and the two resets in `BeginStep`. Kept `ForceDoorClosed` itself — `AccessibilityWatcher.cs:3791` still calls it. Kept `DoorFullyOpenRotationFraction = 0.95f` (still used to detect the stuck pose). **Effect on next sweep:** office doors (`office->hallway`, `office->office_closet`) will now show `door-stuck-player-blocking` in BepInEx logs instead of multi-trigger spam. They may still fail — that's expected, the failures are now honest. (3) **Excluded the bogus `laundry_room↔upper_hallway` pair.** New `$bogusPairs` hashtable at the top of the transitions loop in `Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`, matching the existing `dorian_*` skip pattern. Reasoning: the source graph's input link infers adjacency from XZ proximity, but `laundry_room` (Y≈4.98 / snapped -0.61) and `upper_hallway` (Y≈18.17 / snapped 12.82) are on different floors with no stair or teleporter between them — the runtime can't traverse this as a single step. Confirmed via `dy > 5m` scan: the only other cross-floor transitions are legitimate (`office↔crawlspace` = Teleporter, `hallway_arma↔hallway` = Stairs). Build output went from `transitions=56` to `transitions=54 skippedBogus=1` (one `$link` skip drops both forward and reverse). Stair architecture decision (out of scope but recorded): cross-floor stair routes are categorically different from same-floor transitions and need their own end-to-end traversal harness with a long timeout — deferred. Current 2 `kind=Stairs` failures (`hallway↔upper_hallway`) will stay in the transition sweep as known-failing rows that we ignore until the stair harness lands. Step D (stair Y-lerp fix) from prior plan is dropped — was premature: failures show the player reaches the stair zone fine, so the fix should target traversal-inside-zone, which needs harness data first. **Pending in-game validation:** user to run both sweeps and import; expected door failures 8 → 7 → ~5 after counting Step F-merge passes, open-passage 17 → 15 (the 2 `laundry_room↔upper_hallway` rows are now absent from the harness entirely). **Next session queued work in priority order:** (a) import fresh sweep results, confirm wall-snap landed for `bathroom1↔hallway` and partially for `gym->gym_closet`; (b) **Step F** — read BepInEx LogOutput.log for `door-stuck-player-blocking` lines on the office doors to confirm whether the failure mode there is mid-swing collision or something upstream (door not even firing Interact); (c) **Step E** — open-ended investigation of T5 interact-timeouts (`kitchen->kitchen_tutorial`, `living_room->living_room_tutorial`, `office->office_1love`); 30-min budget per plan; (d) **Stair end-to-end harness** if same-floor work stabilizes. **Code touchpoints for next session:** `scripts\Build-NavigationGraph.ps1` (wall-snap helpers near top, `$bogusPairs` near line 1402, `Get-WallSnappedPoint` call near line 1010 in the `"Door"` post-snap-floor block); `SimpleNavBridge.cs` (TryOpenActiveDoorIfNeeded with new skip reason); plan file at `C:\Users\amock\.claude\plans\navigation-broad-residuals.md` still source of truth but Steps C/D are superseded by today's work.

**Current session note (2026-05-18 continued — plan steps B/F + dorian pivot + Bucket 1 + reverts):** Extended the session beyond Step A. Executed and then **reverted** Step B (5 dorian `$stepMetadata` reclassifications in `scripts\Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`) after user insight: dorian_* cameraspaces are dateable spawn points (Scale=0 point-zones), not navigable destinations. Confirmed against game code — `Door.noderequired()` returning `"dorian_door"` is an unrelated dialogue node-lock string; `dorian_*` CameraSpaces are spawn positions for dateables that appear when Dateviators are equipped and a charge fires. Long-term goal: navigate to every interactable/dateable. MVP scope: room-to-room only — once in the room, the player can find the object. Acted on this by adding a **dorian filter** at the top of the transitions loop in `Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`: skips any link where FromZone or ToZone matches `dorian_*`, logged as `skippedDorian=N`. Result: graph dropped from 112 to 56 transitions (28 skipped, plus their reverse duplicates). Also executed Step F (D2 office-door investigation) — added one-shot per-step entry-dump in `SimpleNavBridge.TryOpenActiveDoorIfNeeded` (door name, open, range, visualRotDelta, moving, dist, playerPos) and door-clearance spawn log in `AccessibilityWatcher.TryGetDoorTransitionSweepSpawnPosition`. Logs read from `D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\Common\Date Everything\BepInEx\LogOutput.log` **disproved the plan's Class D2 hypothesis**: `office -> hallway`'s Doors_Office opens cleanly to its full 80° range, no stuck-recovery loop fires. Actual failure mode is the *open* door panel clipping the threshold-advance corridor — same root cause as the original Class D1 wall failures (gym->gym_closet hits SM_Walls_Gym similarly). **This collapses Step F into Step C** (corridor widening / opposite-lateral-side selection). Kept **Bucket 1** as a general robustness win: added `SimpleNav.FindDoorByName(connectorName)` and made `SimpleNav.TryResolveRoute` + `AccessibilityWatcher.cs:3765` (door-sweep step) prefer `step.ConnectorName` / `step.ConnectorNames` lookup before falling back to AABB matching — exact name beats AABB regardless of dorian context (the AABB resolver could previously pick a wrong nearby door if multiple doors were close to two zones). Reverted **Bucket 2** (dorian-source spawn-zone-mismatch workaround in `BuildDoorTransitionSweepStandClearPosition`) — once dorians are filtered, no path triggers it. Also implemented Step A's `-WriteOverrides` switch on `Import-TransitionSweepReport.ps1` (default off) so imports no longer auto-mutate `navigation_transition_overrides.json`; validated by running an import and confirming the file stays clean in git. Build/deploy verified end-to-end: `dotnet build` `0 warnings / 0 errors`, `Deploy-Mod.ps1` succeeded, graph re-exported with `zones=57 nodes=139 transitions=56 skippedDorian=28`. **Pending validation:** restart game, rerun both door + transition sweeps, import. Expected: ~19 dorian-related failures vanish from both sweeps; pre-existing real failures remain visible (bathroom1↔hallway, attic↔upper_hallway, gym↔gym_closet, office→hallway, office→office_closet — all Class D1/D2-now-merged corridor-clipping; the two stair failures hallway↔upper_hallway; the T2 wall-clipping cases; the T5 interact-timeouts kitchen→kitchen_tutorial / living_room→living_room_tutorial / office→office_1love). **Next session work queued (in priority order):** (1) **Step D** — stair Y-lerp fix in `Get-ConnectorGeometry`'s Stairs case at `Build-NavigationGraph.ps1:836-840`. Current logic does pure linear Lerp-Vec3 between fromWaypoint and toWaypoint at T=0.4/0.6, producing mid-air Y values (e.g. hallway Y=-0.61 ↔ upper_hallway Y=12.82 yields T=0.4 at Y=4.72 and T=0.6 at Y=7.43, neither on any stair tread or landing). Fix: route the lerp via `hallway_arma` (the stair landing, preserved at Y=9.95 by the Step 1 carve-out) as a midpoint instead of a single linear lerp. Sweep evidence: `upper_hallway -> hallway` was routing to `(22.62, 4.35, 2.84)` (wrong part of map); `hallway -> upper_hallway` to Y=17.97 (above upper floor). (2) **Step C** — corridor widening / opposite-lateral-side selection in `Get-ConnectorGeometry` (`$clearDistance` currently 0.8 for doors at `Build-NavigationGraph.ps1:810`). Now covers more than the plan said because Step F collapsed into it: addresses D1 walls, T2 walls, AND D2 office doors (open-panel clipping). Try 1.2m first; if failures persist with similar blockers try 1.6m; if they shift to a *different* wall, switch to opposite-lateral-side strategy. (3) **Step E** — open-ended investigation of TransitionInteract timeouts for T5 (kitchen→kitchen_tutorial, living_room→living_room_tutorial, office→office_1love). Plan said 30-min budget then defer if no obvious fix. Plan file at `C:\Users\amock\.claude\plans\navigation-broad-residuals.md` remains source of truth. **Current session note (2026-05-16 task C — OcclusionPortal integration):** Extended `scripts/Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1` to parse `!u!41 OcclusionPortal` sections. New parser case captures `m_GameObject`, `m_Center`, `m_Size`, `m_Open`; emit step converts local Center → world (rotated and scaled by parent transform) and local Size → world (componentwise scaled by world scale, rotation intentionally not folded into the AABB on this first cut because most doors in this scene are axis-aligned and downstream consumers only need the doorway-opening center for alignment). Parent-Door resolution walks up the transform parent chain looking for any GameObject whose Id matches a `DoorObjects` entry; all 16 portals resolved (each portal sits directly on its Door GameObject). Output schema additions: top-level `OcclusionPortals` array and `Counts.OcclusionPortals`. Also patched the script to accept the current graph schema where `Transitions` replaced the legacy `Links` field. Regenerated `artifacts/navigation/thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json` and added a csproj copy target that ships it alongside the DLL to `BepInEx/plugins/thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json`. Wired `SimpleNav.cs`: added a lazy-loaded `Dictionary<string, SimpleNavDoorPortal>` keyed by door GameObject name (loaded via `JsonUtility.FromJson` from the plugin path, behind `CS0649` suppression for the DTO fields), `TryGetDoorPortal(Door)` resolves it from `door.gameObject.name`, and `TryResolveDoorApproach` projects the portal center onto the lateral axis (XZ perpendicular to `fromSourceDir`) and snaps the approach to that lateral coordinate when a portal is present. The legacy swing-arc lateral-sign heuristic is preserved as fallback for the ~14 real doors without portal data. **Build verified**: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` succeeded `0 warnings / 0 errors`. No runtime testing yet — `Navigation.UseSimpleNavigation` defaults to `false`, so legacy behavior is unchanged; the new portal path only activates when the bridge is wired into autowalk (the post-A/B integration milestone). Next sessions: task A (MeshCollider footprints) then task B (`SimpleNavSceneData.cs`), then flip the bridge into autowalk and rerun the door + open-passage sweeps.

**Current session note (2026-05-16 navigation redesign foundation):** Stepped back from per-failure patching and audited the system end-to-end. Key findings: (1) the game ships no NavMesh — `artifacts/navigation/navmesh_export.live.json` confirms `HasActiveNavMesh: false`, so all "nav graph" data is mod-generated and can be replaced with live reads; (2) the authoritative game-side zone system is `CameraSpaces` (a `Singleton<CameraSpaces>` of `triggerzone` AABBs by name) — the mod has been duplicating that with a graph file that drifts; (3) **systematic y-coordinate bug**: 19 of 22 door transitions in `navigation_graph.generated.json` have `SourceClearPoint.y` / `DestinationClearPoint.y` set to the door's hinge y (e.g. -0.617) instead of the player's floor y (e.g. 4.17), so legacy autowalk targets clip through the floor and trigger the loop detector — this almost certainly explains the bulk of the 13 door / 17 open-passage sweep failures; (4) several door connectors record 12-16m between from/to (they're zone centres, not door endpoints); (5) the 17 open-passage failures include 10 virtual destinations (`dorian_*`, `*_tutorial`, `*_1love`) that can never pass under the current zone-arrival criterion. Foundation added (~700 lines, behind config flag): `SimpleNav.cs` (live `CameraSpaces` + live `FindObjectsOfType<Door>()` + per-zone floor-Y cache + polymorphic `HasArrived` for physical / virtual / dorian destinations + zero-graph-file `TryResolveTarget`), `SimpleNavBridge.cs` (opt-in glue, target resolved once per step rather than per frame, local `Physics.SphereCast` obstacle-sidestep steering for static furniture), `ModConfig.UseSimpleNavigation` flag, single-line tick in `AccessibilityWatcher.Update`. **Build verified**: `dotnet build` succeeded `0 warnings / 0 errors`; legacy code paths untouched. Discussed using the asset export ([thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json](artifacts/navigation/thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json) and [thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json](artifacts/navigation/thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json)) — confirmed valuable stable data: `GraphLinks` (56 pre-computed zone adjacencies), `CameraSpaces.InNavigationGraph` flag (which 57 of 169 zones are "real rooms"), 30 doors with `DoorComponent`, `Teleporters` with authored target positions/rotations. Known gap in `thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json`: `MeshCollidersUnsupported: true` skips 2,317 MeshColliders. User confirmed the original skip-meshes decision was driven by valid concern that AABB-based mesh inclusion would block walkable space (floors, ceiling beams, L-shape negative space). Mitigation discussed: existing `BottomY`/`TopY` band filter already catches floors/ceilings; remaining concave-shape problem is solved by slicing mesh triangles at player chest height and projecting only the intersecting triangles onto XZ to compute the real footprint. Also identified: `!u!41 OcclusionPortal` (16 entries in raw scene YAML) gives the exact 3D doorway opening for ~half the doors — that's the geometric primitive `SimpleNav.TryResolveDoorApproach` is currently faking with swing-arc heuristics.

**Current session note (2026-05-13 open-passage progress-candidate patch):** Applied a narrow open-passage planner fix in `AccessibilityWatcher.OpenPassageNavigation.cs` using same runtime-physics/local-occupancy lens that recovered the recent door rows. For override-only open-passage local planning contexts (`open-passage-override-source` and `open-passage-override-destination`), `ResolveOpenPassageReachablePlanningGoal(...)` now scans the current guided override route from the active waypoint forward and selects the farthest guided waypoint that still has a local path inside the player's current physics-sampled connected component before falling back to the generic nearest reachable proxy. **Current session note (2026-05-13 runtime movement probe):** Investigated the game's runtime movement authority before changing behavior. `BetterPlayerControl` is the player authority, uses `Rigidbody` plus `Collider`, consumes private `move` and `look` fields in `FixedUpdate()`, applies horizontal movement by assigning `Rigidbody.velocity`, and has no explicit horizontal `CanMove` or blocked helper. Added diagnostic-only `Runtime movement probe` logging in `AccessibilityWatcher.cs` on auto-walk loop detector and progress-timeout failures. The probe casts from live player position toward the failed target with the player collider shape when possible, logs the first non-trigger blocker and first trigger hit with object path, collider type, layer, bounds, and hit point, and logs whether the last auto-walk input appears applied but cancelled by low Rigidbody velocity/displacement. Documented the authority/probe details in `docs/game-api.md`. Verification: `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` succeeded with `0` warnings / `0` errors, then `.\scripts\Deploy-Mod.ps1 -Configuration Debug` succeeded after elevation.
