**Current session note (2026-05-07 open-passage top-failure RCA from live audit artifacts):** Investigated the three currently open open-passage failures directly from `artifacts\navigation\live_navigation_audit.live.json`, `transition_sweep.live.json`, `navigation_graph.generated.json`, `local_navigation_maps.generated.json`, `navigation_transition_overrides.json`, and `AccessibilityWatcher.OpenPassageNavigation.cs`. The top two failures `living_room -> hallway` and `hallway -> living_room` are not currently override-backed rows; there are no matching entries in `navigation_transition_overrides.json`, so they run on pure generated `CameraSpaceBoundaryGeometry` data. Both fail as `rawContext=open-passage-guided` with `localContext=<null>`, and the live audit proves the last raw guided target is outside the active source-zone local-map bounds: `living_room -> hallway` ends at `(6.72, 4.35, -6.06)` outside `living_room` by `0.57` on `z`, while `hallway -> living_room` ends at `(7.55, 4.67, -7.13)` outside `hallway` by `1.49` on `z`. Code review confirms that once open-passage traversal reaches `DestinationWaypoint` or `DestinationHandoff`, source-side local planning survives only if `TryResolveOpenPassageDestinationStageSourceBridgeLocalNavigationGoal(...)` can still return a live bridge; otherwise `TryResolveOpenPassageLocalNavigationGoal(...)` falls straight back to raw guided movement and logs `Skipped open-passage source local planning after source handoff`. Practical implication: next work for the living-room pair should audit graph waypoint source and local-map coverage first, not add another generic executor heuristic. `dining_room -> piano_room` is now a different lane: it still has active local steering (`localContext=open-passage-override-destination`) and fails as a true no-progress local loop on `LocalWaypoint (-17.90, 4.84, -9.88)`, so it is lower-value than the structural living-room pair despite still being open. No code changes in this pass.

**Current session note (2026-05-07 living-room waypoint-source and local-map coverage audit):** Audited the generated `living_room <-> hallway` rows in `artifacts\navigation\navigation_graph.generated.json`, local-map envelopes in `artifacts\navigation\local_navigation_maps.generated.json`, live sweep evidence in `artifacts\navigation\transition_sweep.live.json`, and the source-bridge gate in `AccessibilityWatcher.OpenPassageNavigation.cs`. Findings are now sharper than the earlier RCA. Both directions are pure `CameraSpaceBoundaryGeometry` rows with `StaticSuspicionScore = 0` and no matching override entry, so the executor has no authored intermediate bridge data to preserve after source handoff. The generated points already cross beyond the source-zone envelopes exactly where the live failures happen: for `living_room -> hallway`, `DestinationClearPoint (9.56, 4.35, -5.60)` sits `1.03` units beyond `living_room` max `z=-6.63`, and final `ToWaypoint (12.06, 4.35, -0.44)` sits `6.19` beyond; for `hallway -> living_room`, `DestinationClearPoint (5.10, 4.67, -6.70)` sits `1.06` beyond `hallway` min `z=-5.64`, and final `ToWaypoint (5.28, 4.67, -13.19)` sits `7.55` beyond. Live sweep targets are the progressive raw guided points between those off-zone graph points, which is why they still fail slightly outside the source envelopes at `(6.72, 4.35, -6.06)` and `(7.55, 4.67, -7.13)`. Important code constraint: `TryResolveOpenPassageDestinationStageSourceBridgeLocalNavigationGoal(...)` only runs for override-only guidance. For these pure generated rows, `TryResolveOpenPassageLocalNavigationGoal(...)` intentionally drops source local steering once traversal reaches destination-stage guidance, so `localContext=<null>` here is expected behavior, not a separate regression. Practical implication: highest-value fix lane is data/modeling. Either add authored override-only intermediate waypoints for this pair so source-bridge local planning can stay alive, or expand/reassign local-map coverage around the living-room/hallway opening so the generated destination-clear corridor is still representable from the source side. No code change, build, or deploy in this audit pass.

**Current session note (2026-05-07 higher-level RCA: source-zone local map envelope gap, not just executor branching):** Imported artifact remains the latest user-provided proof state `871342531B36C69057959443B0FFD3A194C25F3452A053994A5444155EB6B7D1` at `22 total / 20 passed / 2 failed / 0 pending`, with only `gym_closet -> gym` and `office -> office_closet` failing. Analysis focused on whether those two routes are missing usable local-navigation data rather than still needing another executor tweak. Current evidence says yes, but in a specific way: the failing source-zone retained-extended targets sit outside the source zone envelopes that `LocalNavigationMaps` can actually plan inside. `gym_closet` bounds are `x=-3.392..3.052, z=8.241..27.669`, but the retained extended target is `(-6.11, 12.82, 22.66)`, over `2.7` units outside the source envelope on `x`; the log shows that target being snapped back to `(-3.14, 12.82, 22.49)`, then immediately treated as a completed one-point local plan even while raw extended distance is still about `2.8`. `office` bounds are `x=5.409..20.861, z=7.394..27.046`, but the retained extended target `(2.51, -0.62, 24.86)`, final raw `EntryWaypoint` `(-1.07, 4.17, 23.96)`, and even source clear point `(4.84, -0.62, 25.98)` all sit outside the office source envelope on `x`; log proof shows source-local replans succeeding only to interior office waypoints around `x≈5.66`, then looping forever because the real doorway/closet targets are unreachable to the source-zone planner. By contrast, working closet exits such as `bedroom_closet -> bedroom` and `laundry_room_closet -> laundry_room` keep their source-clear points inside the source zone envelopes. Practical implication: after many branch-level fixes, remaining two failures now look architectural. Problem is not absence of all local data, but missing doorway/interstitial coverage in source-zone local map geometry or zone-envelope assignment for these two closet families. Next step should not be another executor heuristic. Better lanes are `(1)` expand or reassign local map coverage so the source planner can legally represent those doorway/clearance targets, or `(2)` change door planning contract so post-interaction planning can temporarily use shared/interstitial or destination-side data once proof completes, instead of forcing impossible source-zone local plans.

**Current session note (2026-05-07 local/deployed split fix for gym raw-loop arming and office post-release targeting):** Applied one narrow split patch on top of the latest log-backed two-failure RCA. In `AccessibilityWatcher.cs`, retained-extended no-progress release is no longer armed only from local-proxy loop release. `RecordDoorPostInteractionLoopDetection(...)` now also arms `_doorRetainedExtendedNoProgressReleaseStepKey` directly from raw post-interaction loop exhaustion when the exact focused closet retained-extended shape is present: source-zone door step, raw context `door-entry-advance-extended`, target kind `ZoneFallback`, post-threshold committed, and both `door-entry-advance-extended-local` plus `door-entry-advance-local` already complete. A new narrow helper, `TryResolveReleasedDoorFinalEntryLocalNavigationGoal(...)`, now tries to rebuild a source-local `door-entry-advance-local` plan to the explicit final target and only uses it when both a planning goal and planning zone resolve. This targets `office -> office_closet`, where the old release path correctly escaped retained-extended deadlock but then fell straight into unreachable raw `EntryWaypoint` targeting from `office5`. **Current session note (2026-04-30 vertical modeling):** Reviewed multi-floor handling after the user called out stairs and attic/upper-hallway vertical layout. Current mod is partially height-aware: graph and semantic points keep world `y`, static validation flags `LargeHeightDelta`, transitions can be `Stairs`, and local-map blocker generation filters blockers against each zone's 3D camera-space bounds so overlapping floors do not share blockers. Runtime local navigation is still 2D per zone and `Stairs` currently borrow the open-passage guided model when manual waypoints exist. Next semantic-contract work should add explicit vertical connector metadata such as floor/height band, connector subtype, lower/upper landings, accepted intermediate zones, and a rule for when local planning is source-floor, connector, or destination-floor.

**Current session note (2026-04-30 RCA):** RCA split the six `46893E8D...` failures into three fix families. First, `dining_room -> piano_room` and `living_room -> hallway` no longer reuse stale `open-passage-override-source` after source handoff, but `TryResolveOpenPassageLocalNavigationGoal(...)` now returns `false` for `DestinationWaypoint` and `DestinationHandoff` while the player is still in an accepted source subzone; both routes then chase raw `EntryWaypoint` targets with `localContext=<null>`, and `living_room -> hallway` also has `BeaconSnapsFar` on source clear/crossing. Patch target: add a destination-stage source-zone bridge/proxy for override-guided open passages, without restoring the stale source proxy. Second, `dorian_irondoor -> bedroom_closet` is not a normal flat open passage: semantic static suspicion is `8` from `LongWaypointSpan`, `LargeHeightDelta`, `WideCrossingGap`, far destination clear/approach, and far destination waypoint; runtime starts in `kitchen4`, moves through kitchen-family zones, and times out away from `bedroom_closet`. Patch target: remodel or mark that row special/deferred unless a real connector path is identified. Third, door failures come from post-interaction state/proxy mismatch: terminal `door-threshold-advance-local` paths can be marked complete around `1.57` to `1.97` meters while raw threshold arrival is `0.50`, so `bathroom1 -> hallway` and `office -> hallway` oscillate threshold/handoff or loop on source-side local waypoints; `office -> office_closet` reaches `DoorEntryAdvanceExtended`, but `door-entry-advance-local` snaps back to the source threshold, is discarded for `forwardProgress=0.00`, and leaves a raw `ZoneFallback (2.51, -0.62, 24.86)` with `localContext=<null>`. Patch target: separate local proxy completion from raw threshold release, then add a real extended-entry bridge that cannot snap backward to the source threshold.

## Game Key Bindings (Original)

- Documented in `docs/game-api.md`
- Game input is primarily action-based through Rewired rather than hardcoded `KeyCode` checks
- Hardcoded keys found so far and reserved: `F2`, `F3`, `F8`

## Implemented Features

- Startup announcement through Tolk/NVDA
- `F1` help hotkey
- `F6` current-room report with facing-relative object grouping
- `Ctrl+F1` repeat-last-spoken hotkey for tips and dialogue lines
- `Ctrl+F6` current-objective tracking hotkey
- `Ctrl+Shift+F6` whole-house room list hotkey
- `Ctrl+Alt+F6` tracked-object auto-walk hotkey
- `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F6` door transition sweep hotkey
- `F9` debug toggle hotkey
- `Ctrl+F9` accessibility settings hotkey
- `Ctrl+Shift+F9` runtime navmesh export hotkey
- `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F9` automated transition sweep hotkey
- Spoken accessibility settings menu with persistent toggles for focused items, dialogue text, dialogue choices, screen text, phone app text, room changes, nearby objects, and status changes
- Automatic speech for focused UI controls
- Automatic speech for active dialogue lines
- Automatic speech for dialogue choice focus
- Arrow-key dialogue choice navigation
- Arrow-key chat reply navigation for active `ParallelChat.Options`
- `Enter` / `Space` choice activation
- `ValidateQuestions` focus speech for name, town, favorite thing, pronoun toggles, and confirmation toggle
- Automatic screen summaries for phone/menu contexts
- Consolidated phone app content announcements through one current-app reader with visible-text fallback
- Automatic object examination text announcements
- Automatic room announcements in house exploration
- Automatic nearby interactable announcements
- Automatic Dateviators equip/charge state announcements
- Room navigation routing based on `navigation_graph.json` waypoints plus live `CameraSpaces` zone positions
- Auto-walk guidance and directional object-tracker beeps for the tracked object or current navigation waypoint, with pitch tied to camera-relative vertical position, cadence tied to proximity, and volume tied to proximity
- Transition-aware auto-walk with teleporter waits, connector interaction retries, and stronger left or right tracker cues
- Live navmesh export to `BepInEx\plugins\navmesh_export.live.json` with repo-side import support through `.\scripts\Import-NavMeshExport.ps1`
- Live selected-object coverage export to `BepInEx\plugins\selected_object_coverage.live.json` with repo-side import support through `.\scripts\Import-SelectedObjectCoverageReport.ps1`
- Asset-side scene navmesh inspection through `.\scripts\Inspect-SceneNavMeshAssets.ps1`, with report written to `artifacts\navigation\thirdpersongreybox-navmesh-assets-report.json`
- Static transition validation through `.\scripts\Inspect-NavigationTransitions.ps1`, with report written to `artifacts\navigation\transition_validation.static.json`
- Static door-transition metadata audit through `.\scripts\Inspect-DoorTransitionMetadata.ps1`, with report written to `artifacts\navigation\door_transition_audit.json`
- Dev-only automated transition sweep through `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F9`, with repo-side import support through `.\scripts\Import-TransitionSweepReport.ps1`
- Dev-only automated door transition sweep through `Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F6`, with repo-side import support through `.\scripts\Import-DoorTransitionSweepReport.ps1`
- Tone-versus-movement diagnostics in navigation debug output so tracker drift or delayed waypoint updates can be distinguished from pathfinding failures

Verification steps: `.\scripts\Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`, `.\scripts\Inspect-NavigationTransitions.ps1`, and `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` all succeeded; the fresh static report currently counts `112` total transitions, `74` heuristic-suspicious transitions, and `18` high-suspicion transitions.
- Passed locally on 2026-04-04: made tracked-object routing position-aware instead of only zone-aware. `NavigationGraph.FindPathSteps(...)` now biases the first step from the player's live position and the final step toward the tracked object's live position while preserving the exact chosen link during reconstruction, and `AccessibilityWatcher` now requests a refresh when that live search picks a different first step even if the player is still in the same source zone. Same-family runtime subzones such as `office2` and `office6` now keep direct-object guidance instead of falling back to a room anchor, and tracked-object arrival checks now treat equivalent subzones as the same room. `dotnet build .\DateEverythingAccess.csproj --no-restore -p:SkipCopyToPlugins=true` is the verification step to rerun after this patch.

## Known Issues

- No public source repository was found for Date Everything!, so this project uses the separate-mod workflow.
- The game relies heavily on Rewired action maps, so default physical keyboard keys are not all visible from code alone.
- The decompiled `TranslationManager` appears to ignore non-English text output in this build, so mod localization still needs its own fallback behavior.

## Execution Plan

1. Finish transition reliability first.
   - Clear the two remaining hard blockers: `dining_room -> piano_room` and `office -> office_closet`.
   - Add sweep-grade validation for `Stairs` and `Teleporter` steps instead of leaving them to ad hoc runtime checks.
   - Exit criterion: no unresolved directed transition failures in imported runtime reports for open passages, doors, stairs, or the crawlspace teleporter, so a selected target is not unreachable from another room because of a broken house transition.

2. Build an internal object coverage registry.
   - Export and persist a blind-friendly list of navigable interactables with stable id, spoken label, scene object name, resolved zone, graph-zone normalization, and candidate approach metadata.
   - Mark coverage failures explicitly when an interactable lacks a usable label, zone, or approach target instead of silently dropping it.
   - This is internal validation data; the current requirement is that any selected object be navigable, not that the player must be able to browse every object through a new house-wide picker.
   - Exit criterion: every house object we care about is either navigable with metadata or listed in a concrete failure report.

3. Replace heuristic final-arrival fallback where needed.
   - Keep the current bounds-based candidate generation as a fallback, but add authored or audited approach targets for objects that routinely stop at bad pivots or blocked sides.
   - Treat raw-object fallback as diagnostic-only once audited approach data exists for a target family.
   - Exit criterion: tracked-object arrival stops at a usable interaction position for the audited object set, not merely near the object pivot and not at the wrong side of the target.

4. Keep target-selection work optional and out of the critical path.
   - Do not block navigation readiness on a new house-wide picker unless later requested.
   - Only add selection UX work if the existing objective-tracking and current-room picking paths prove too limited for real gameplay.
   - Exit criterion: current selection paths can hand off valid targets to navigation reliably; any future global picker is a follow-up feature, not a prerequisite.

5. Add coverage-first diagnostics and reports.
   - Add scripts or runtime dumps that report object coverage, missing labels, missing zones, raw-object fallbacks, and failed approach-target resolution.
   - Also report loop, stall, and wrong-destination outcomes during auto-walk so end-to-end failures are distinguishable from simple "no path" cases.
   - Add tracker diagnostics that compare the tone target against the active movement target, local-waypoint context, and current step so tone drift or delayed waypoint updates are visible in one place.
   - Keep the outputs importable into `artifacts\navigation\` so the next pass can be evidence-driven instead of heuristic-driven.
   - Exit criterion: missing-data cases are visible in one report instead of discovered only during gameplay.

6. Run end-to-end verification against real gameplay tasks.
   - Verify room scan, room-object picker, tutorial objective tracking, tracked-object auto-walk, stairs, teleporter traversal, and final interaction arrival in runtime from multiple starting rooms and floors.
   - Verify tone-path coherence in runtime: when the player centers the tone and moves forward, movement should follow the same current path segment, and the tone should update immediately when the active waypoint or local navigation target changes.
   - Re-test after any graph, override, or local-map regeneration.
   - Exit criterion: repeated runtime passes show that from anywhere in the house, a selected covered object can be reached without stalling, looping, or leading the player to the wrong place, and the navigation tone remains aligned with that live route throughout the trip.

## Architecture Decisions

- `AGENTS.md` is the primary control file for Codex. `CLAUDE.md` and `CLAUDE.de.md` are compatibility notices only.
- Use `BepInEx` for this project because current public Date Everything! mods target BepInEx.
- Use `ILSpy CLI` for a terminal-first, automatable, screen-reader-friendly decompilation workflow.
- Plan multilingual support from the start with `Loc.cs`.
- Prefer CLI-driven installation steps when possible, asking for approval when elevated access or network access is required.
- Base framework verified: BepInEx loads `Date Everything Access 0.1.0`, NVDA is detected, and the startup announcement path runs.
- Use `ControllerMenuUI` as the authoritative focus writer because it enforces the same navigation rules as the game UI.
- Use reflection against `TalkingUI` and `DialogBoxBehavior` for dialogue text and choice extraction until a cleaner event path is needed.

## Key Bindings (Mod)

- F1: Help
- Ctrl+F1: Repeat last spoken line
- Ctrl+F6: Track the current tutorial objective
- Ctrl+Shift+F6: Open and cycle the whole-house room list
- Ctrl+Alt+F6: Toggle auto-walk to the tracked object
- F9: Toggle debug mode
- Ctrl+F9: Accessibility settings

## Notes for Next Session

- Analysis note on 2026-04-16: reliable direct-to-object guidance does not appear blocked on one missing hidden game field. The bigger gaps are still engineering and validation: finish transition reliability, keep coarse normalized zones such as `hallway` from collapsing disconnected local-map components, replace heuristic final-arrival fallbacks with audited approach targets for problem objects, and keep exporting whole-house object coverage so missing labels, zones, or approach data are explicit. Extra game data would still help in two targeted places if it can be found or derived: authoritative per-object interaction or approach anchors and richer blocker geometry for in-room avoidance. The existing generic `Interactable.interactedPosition` field is not that anchor; decompiled code shows it is written from `positionWhenInteracting`, and many serialized values in scene exports are still zero, so it should be treated as runtime state rather than trustworthy authored arrival metadata.
- Feasibility review on 2026-04-16: the current navigation stack is substantially closer to reliable room-level travel than to reliable direct-to-object arrival. Room discovery and current-room object enumeration already exist, while exact-object auto-walk still depends on heuristic zone resolution, exact runtime-zone `DirectObject` handoff, and component-aware final approach inside coarse multi-component spaces such as `hallway`. If progress stays slow after the fresh reruns, the lowest-risk scope cut is to make whole-house navigation room-first: pick a room from a graph-zone list, auto-walk to that room, treat arrival at an equivalent room-family subzone as success, then use the existing `F6` room report for the final local search. Keep direct-to-object auto-walk as a second-phase enhancement rather than the primary definition of "navigation done."
- The authoritative full reruns from runtime build `44EEC894...` widened the real blocker set to `10` open passages and `9` doors. The current repo patch addresses two generic causes only: override-only open-passage local planning now follows the same progressive guided subtarget as live movement, and door threshold handoff now rejects snapped bridge points that make no real forward progress away from the source threshold. This patch is now deployed; the next authoritative proof step is a fresh full open sweep plus a fresh full door sweep from that deployed build before any more route data is trusted.
- On the `2026-04-11` import review, the first actionable log clue was `Failed to load navigation transition overrides` before the open sweep failures. That was caused by single-item `AcceptedSourceZones` or `AcceptedDestinationZones` being serialized as scalar strings instead of arrays, which meant the live `living_room -> hallway` and `dining_room -> piano_room` retunes were not actually loading. Both the importer and the runtime loader now tolerate and rewrite that shape; do not trust any new open-sweep failure analysis until the reruns come from the post-fix deployed build.
- User-confirmed follow-up on `2026-04-10`: the short ladder-climb cutscene played in both `crawlspace -> office` and `office -> crawlspace`, and the generic transition sweep had to be run three times to finish the full report. Treat both teleporter failures in the imported open-sweep artifact as false negatives caused by the cutscene interrupting the sweep state machine, not as live traversal blockers. If the sweep harness is revisited later, it should treat that ladder cutscene plus the temporary zone drift into `office_closet` as success instead of timeout.
- `dining_room -> piano_room` and `living_room -> hallway` now share the same failure shape: source-side local planning stays active and returns `LocalWaypoint` targets, but the player stalls about `1.13` to `1.17` units from the snapped threshold goal until recovery is exhausted. That means the current blocker is no longer the old generic `2f` arrival rule; it is the authored threshold target geometry itself. Next implementation should snap those source-handoff or override checkpoints onto truly reachable walkable cells, or add a bounded stall-aware advance rule, before increasing timeouts again.
- `bathroom2 -> dorian_bathroom2_1`, `bedroom -> dorian_bedroom1`, and `hallway -> hallway_arma` now look more like graph-shape or transition-classification problems than ordinary open-passage tuning. In all three cases the source-side handoff runs, then the step advances to an off-zone destination or `EntryWaypoint` target and local navigation immediately drops out. Treat these first as door-camera or vertical-connector modeling issues before adding more open-passage waypoints.
- The remaining door failures all still happen after interaction succeeds. `bedroom -> bathroom2`, `hallway -> bathroom1`, `hallway -> office`, `office -> hallway`, `office -> office_closet`, and `upper_hallway -> gym` stall because `DoorThresholdAdvance` or `DoorThresholdHandoff` emits a raw `ZoneFallback` target and local planning drops out, leaving the player short of the last walkable source-side cell. Next implementation should keep source-zone door movement locked onto the snapped walkable proxy until threshold clearance is proven, instead of handing movement back to the raw handoff point too early.
- `selected_object_coverage.live.json` is finally trustworthy and it points away from `ObjectTracker` as the active bug for the current `Magnifying Glass` target. Selected-object resolution passed, approach snapping passed, and `TrackerAlignment` reports `TargetDelta = 0`; the large failure counts are downstream of the hallway, office, and crawlspace transition blockers plus coarse-zone arrival targeting. Clear those route-proof gaps before spending more time on tracker audio or tracker target alignment.
- On the `2026-04-09` reruns summarized at `04:39:07` and `04:38:57`, `dining_room -> piano_room` no longer fails because local planning is absent. It now fails because the new local-planning branch and the old arrival cutoff disagree: `open-passage-override-source` keeps the path alive until `0.75f`, but `AutoWalkArrivalDistance` still marks the returned `LocalWaypoint` as reached at `2f`. With the player about `1.13` units from the snapped threshold cell, auto-walk repeatedly "arrives" at the same local waypoint and recomputes it instead of actually closing the gap. Next implementation should give local-waypoint arrival a tighter context-aware cutoff or otherwise stop the generic `2f` arrival rule from short-circuiting this override path.
- On the same reruns, `office -> office_closet` still leaves `DoorThresholdAdvance` and stays in `DoorPushThrough`, so the old source-threshold deadlock remains fixed. The new blocker is that the intended `door-push-through` local steering still never shows up in the log: there is no `Local navigation target kind=LocalWaypoint` for this step, only the raw push-through point `(3.24, -0.62, 25.90)` plus repeated `Transition interaction skipped due to retry cooldown` until the step times out in `office5`. Next implementation should make source-zone door push-through either produce a real local waypoint or snap the push-through point onto a walkable target, and it should confirm that retry cooldown is not preventing the last recovery attempt.
- The raw open-passage report still serializes `ValidationTimeoutSeconds` from the graph step, not the live override timeout. Do not treat the rerun's `ValidationTimeoutSeconds = 5` field as evidence that the live `StepTimeoutSeconds = 10` override failed to load; runtime timeout enforcement already reads the override path.
- On the `2026-04-09T01:45:57Z` and `2026-04-09T01:46:18Z` cached reruns, the raw live files again contain only the unresolved subset: `dining_room -> piano_room` for open passages and `office -> office_closet` for doors. That older repo-side import flow merged rerun fragments back into the historical totals, which is why the archived notes from that point still refer to `80` open passages with `79` passed and `1` failed plus `22` doors with `21` passed and `1` failed.
- The current live `dining_room -> piano_room` override in `D:\SteamLibrary\steamapps\Common\Date Everything\BepInEx\plugins\navigation_transition_overrides.json` now uses the newly deployed walkable-cell route (`UseExplicitCrossingSegments = false`, `StepTimeoutSeconds = 10.0`), and the repo copy matches the live file again after deploy.
- The latest `office -> office_closet` log no longer shows the older post-timeout `Auto-walk recovery succeeded via interaction` line. Treat this as a behavior change: the current rerun dies inside the raw `DoorPushThrough` loop with interaction retries stuck on cooldown, so the next fix should target the active push-through target or the retry path itself rather than only sweep failure-recording timing.
- Code review on 2026-04-09: the current navigation stack is shortest-cost by its own heuristic, not a proof of globally optimal traversal. `NavigationGraph.FindPathSteps(...)` minimizes graph-link cost plus live start/end bias, same-zone `LocalNavigationMaps` uses 8-neighbor A* over the occupancy grid, and tracked-object arrival picks the shortest reachable approach candidate when local maps are available; however, graph link cost is still based on connector waypoint distance rather than full runtime travel time, and the latest imported sweeps still leave `dining_room -> piano_room` and `office -> office_closet` as the two blockers before we can claim strong confidence that waypoint placement is fully correct and efficient end to end.
- With pass-cache enabled, the raw game-side sweep JSONs still describe only the current rerun subset. As of the 2026-04-10 import-script change, the repo-side imported artifacts now mirror that latest rerun instead of merging older entries back into the report, so future summaries should stop showing stale duplicates or superseded failures.
- Importing the fresh open-passage rerun on `2026-04-08` refreshed the repo-side `navigation_transition_overrides.json`, so the repo copy and the live game-side plugin copy no longer match until the overrides file is redeployed. Avoid treating that override drift as evidence that the just-imported sweep itself was stale; it is a post-import repo-side change.
- The generated navigation graph now uses schema version `2` with top-level `Zones`, `Nodes`, and directed `Transitions`; do not assume the old flat `Links` shape when adding tooling.
- Current asset-derived counts are `57` zones, `139` room nodes, and `112` directed transitions. The next runtime sweep or navmesh export should be compared against those directed counts, not the older `56`-transition undirected expectation.
- `TransitionSweepReporter`, `NavMeshExporter`, and `AccessibilityWatcher` now expose or consume explicit connector `SourceApproachPoint`, `SourceClearPoint`, `DestinationClearPoint`, and `DestinationApproachPoint` fields plus per-transition accepted runtime subzones and timeout recommendations. If a runtime failure still occurs, inspect those fields before adding another override.
- Portable release packaging now lives in `.\scripts\Build-PortableRelease.ps1` and `artifacts\release-src\v0.1.0\portable\`; use that path when rebuilding or revising the self-contained loader bundle.
- `navigation_graph.json` is loaded from the game-side `BepInEx\plugins\` directory. The live file was replaced on 2026-04-02 with the generated waypoint graph from `.\scripts\Build-NavigationGraph.ps1`, and the repo copy lives at `artifacts\navigation\navigation_graph.generated.json`.
- The generated graph now includes transition metadata (`StepKind`, `ConnectorName`, `RequiresInteraction`, `TransitionWaitSeconds`) so the runtime can distinguish open passages, doors, stairs, and teleporter links.
- The navigation tracker now maps pitch to target height relative to the camera, beep cadence to true target proximity, and beep volume to proximity; the next in-game pass should confirm that the higher or lower cue and louder-as-you-approach curve remain readable during stairs, upper-floor routes, and crawlspace transitions.
- Navigation asset inputs can now be regenerated locally with `.\scripts\Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1`; the current generated output is in `artifacts\navigation\thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json`, and the curated connector shortlist lives in `artifacts\navigation\README.md`.
- AssetRipper's exported `ThirdPersonGreybox.unity` scene confirms that `CameraSpaces.zones` contains all JSON graph rooms plus many finer-grained room variants. If navigation quality still needs improvement after runtime testing, the next evidence-based step is to refine the open-passage heuristics or promote more extra room variants into explicit intermediate nodes.
- The crawlspace edge is special-cased in assets through `Teleporter.LocationDown`, `Teleporter.LocationUp`, and explicit teleport rotations, so it can be treated as an authored transition instead of an inferred hallway-style edge.
- The runtime navigation code now consumes waypoint steps plus transition metadata from the generated graph, but it still needs in-game verification for door crossings, crawlspace traversal behavior, stairs, open-passage tuning, and final tracked-object handoff once the player enters the destination room.
- The navigation hotkeys now mix room and object targets: `Ctrl+F6` resolves tutorial objectives to live objects, `Ctrl+Shift+F6` cycles the whole-house room list, and `Ctrl+Alt+F6` auto-walks to the currently selected room or tracked object. All three still need in-game verification for target resolution, naming clarity, and blocked-path behavior.
- With `F9` debug mode enabled, the navigation log now emits explicit snapshots for target-source selection, path-refresh reasons, next-target selection, transition interaction search and gating, and auto-walk recovery. The next runtime pass should capture those lines around any `Ctrl+F6`, `Ctrl+Shift+F6`, or `Ctrl+Alt+F6` failure before trying more heuristics.
- The 2026-04-03 looping log initially came from an older game-side DLL hash (`8585EED2...`) rather than the then-current local build. After the later closed-game deploy, the game-side plugin DLL now matches the latest local build at `BA95E3EF52EE72AC12CF27EB27C789DD02CC8CE7CB32D12C1B94767441474124`, so the next log should include the deeper navigation instrumentation.
- Phone app speech now depends on the consolidated current-app announcer path; if a phone regression appears, inspect that generic path before reviving the deleted per-app detail methods.
- The public `v0.1.0` release now has both a minimal mod zip and a portable zip; if one changes, keep the asset names and README contents aligned so users can tell which package already includes BepInEx.
- Public release `v0.1.0` is now live at `https://github.com/ObjectInSpace/date-everything-access/releases/tag/v0.1.0`; if the package layout changes later, rebuild the zip so it still contains `BepInEx\plugins\DateEverythingAccess.dll`, `Tolk.dll`, `nvdaControllerClient64.dll`, and an updated plain-text `README.txt`.
- If chat repeat still fails after the current selection-aware pass, the next step is targeted runtime logging around `RequestRepeatLastSpeech()`, the selected-object branch inside `TryBuildCurrentRepeatableAnnouncement()`, and the final text handed to `ScreenReader.Say(...)` so the mismatch can be narrowed to state detection versus repeat-memory handling.
- `Ctrl+F1` now checks active chat UI through `TryBuildChatAppAnnouncement(...)` before falling back to generic phone-app content, so if chat repeat still fails, inspect whether `ChatMaster` is active in the affected screen and whether the visible transcript or options are actually being built from that path.
- Chat phone-app content is now stored as repeatable speech when the current phone-app content key resolves to a chat context, so future `Ctrl+F1` regressions should inspect the phone-app content key and whether the latest spoken chat came from that path.
- The latest single-choice reply fix is compiled in `bin\Debug\net472\DateEverythingAccess.dll`, but if runtime retesting is needed immediately, close the game first so `.\scripts\Build-Mod.ps1` can replace the locked plugin DLL.
- If chat-app reply focus still fails in runtime after the Win32 key-state and suppression fix, add temporary debug logging for `GetActiveChatChoices()`, the currently selected object chain, and whether `QuickResponseService` is rebuilding or replacing `Options` between frames before trying more watcher-side navigation changes.
- The 2026-04-08 choice-navigation follow-up restores pointer-style watcher focus for dialogue and chat choices and fixes stale chat virtual-index seeding. The next runtime retest should verify both main dialogue choices and `ParallelChat.Options` in Wrkspace, Thiscord, and Canopy before making any more watcher-side input changes.
- Chat-app reply navigation now has a dedicated watcher path that targets `ParallelChat.Options`; if runtime testing still misses focus changes or announcements in Wrkspace, Thiscord, or Canopy, inspect the live selected object and whether another phone-app control is stealing focus after `ControllerMenuUI.SetCurrentlySelected(..., isViaPointer: true)`.
- `ValidateQuestions` now has a narrow, evidence-based focus path again: it reads only the decompiled interactive controls, not the missing printed form art, so future work on that screen should keep the focused-field and pronoun support unless runtime testing disproves it.
- The main-menu `ValidateQuestions` screen exposes its interactive controls in decompiled C#, but the large printed employment-form copy does not appear in the decompiled source; if this screen is revisited, inspect scene assets or runtime-rendered objects instead of retrying watcher-only string extraction.
- `main` is currently aligned with `origin/main`; keep pushing watcher and documentation updates promptly even if some runtime checks are still pending.
- Date A Dex now uses a Harmony `DateADex.OpenEntry(int)` hook plus watcher-side entry suppression so opening a datable speaks the visible biography before entry controls such as `Collectables` are allowed to interrupt.
- Wrkspace contact navigation is currently working again, and the temporary debug-only logging used to track the regressions has been removed from the live build.
- Public GitHub repository is now available at `https://github.com/ObjectInSpace/date-everything-access` and `main` tracks `origin/main`.
- Local and remote git setup are complete; future work can use the normal `git status`, `git commit`, and `git push` flow.
- Spoken settings menu input is now processed from `AccessibilityWatcher.Update()` instead of `Main.Update()`.
- The spoken settings menu now uses a Win32 key-state fallback for arrows and also accepts `Enter` and `Space` to toggle the current option.
- While the spoken settings menu is open, the mod pushes the game's input stack to `None` so Rewired gameplay and UI actions do not consume those keys.
- While the spoken settings menu is open, live text updates continue, but focused-item selection speech stays suppressed to avoid fighting the menu prompts.
- `Ctrl+F1` now prefers the latest dialogue line or loading-screen tip, then falls back to the last general announcement if needed.
- Pressing `F9` now enables debug mode and also dumps the current live Rewired keyboard, mouse, and joystick maps to the BepInEx log so exact physical bindings can be inspected without guessing from action ids alone.
- The input-mapping dump now falls back to Rewired `SystemPlayer` when `Player0` has not yet taken ownership of the active controllers, which covers pre-engagement and menu states more reliably.
- Chat-entry activation now uses chat-specific pending-detail handling in `AccessibilityWatcher`, so selecting a chat thread should defer the button label briefly and then speak the visible transcript or reply options once the active chat panel has settled.
- `Ctrl+F1` repeat requests now get handed off from the background hotkey thread to `AccessibilityWatcher.Update()` so the mod can safely inspect Unity UI state before speaking.
- `Ctrl+F1` now tries the current dialogue or current visible screen-text context first, even when automatic screen text is turned off, and only then falls back to the remembered repeatable line.
- Local build succeeded after the repeat-last-spoken hotkey pass.
- Phone and app text should prefer full on-screen text coverage rather than shorter summaries.
- `docs/game-api.md` now includes an app-by-app phone text source map:
  Roomers uses `RoomersEntryButton` plus `RoomersInfo` and `RoomersTip`.
  Date A Dex uses `DexEntryButton`, direct bio fields, list summary counters, and `CollectablesScreen`.
  Wrkspace, Canopy, and Thiscord render message text from Ink into `ParallelChat` and `ChatTextBox`, with choices coming from `currentChoices`.
  Music uses `MusicEntryButton` and `MusicPlayer.SongTitle`.
  Art uses `ArtEntryButton` plus `ArtPlayer.selectedArt.title`; `ArtTitle` exists but no assignment path was found in the decompiled scan.
  Save/load, settings, specs, credits, and popups expose their own TMP fields and can be handled separately from the main phone app summaries.
  More specifically, `SaveSlot` owns the visible slot metadata in `Name`, `Date`, `Time`, `playTime`, and `daysPlayed`, while `SaveScreenManager` keeps the special `newSaveSlot` entry in a private field and focuses its child button rather than the text container.
- `AccessibilityWatcher` now:
  treats first-time SPECS tutorial dialogs as a short pending sequence instead of only a one-frame open delay, so SPECS summary, glossary or stats detail, SPECS focus speech, and phone-app visible-text fallback all stay suppressed until that tutorial dialog chain has had time to appear and settle,
  applies a short initial SPECS-open grace window before any SPECS summary, glossary, stats, or SPECS focus speech can fire, so the first tutorial `UIDialog` alert has time to appear and speak first,
  gates automatic phone app content speech behind its own `Phone app text` setting instead of the broader `Screen text` toggle, and only suppresses Date A Dex or chat focus chatter when that phone app content setting is enabled,
  consolidates phone app text speech into a single current-app content pass that prefers app-specific readers but falls back to visible TMP text from the live phone app roots, including the extra Roomers and Date A Dex widget roots when those apps are open,
  reads `ExamineController.ExamineText` while `ExamineController.isShown` so object examination text is spoken immediately and available to `Ctrl+F1` repeat,
  suppresses the popup's auto-focused default button briefly after a new popup announcement so the alert title/body is spoken instead of being interrupted by `OK` or `Yes`,
  reads `UIDialogManager` title/body text and briefly suppresses the dialog's auto-focused button so tutorial and utility dialogs do not collapse to `OK`,
  follows the decompiled SPECS source map more closely by reading stats-page speech only from visible `SpecStatBlock` name, value, and adjective fields, reading tooltip descriptions only from active `statTooltips`, reading glossary speech only from visible `SpecGlossaryBlock` entries, giving the initial SPECS button an explicit descriptive label instead of its raw glyph text, and briefly suppressing the default SPECS focus target so the content can finish speaking,
  announces credits from `CreditsScreen.tmp_credits` using the visible masked text instead of only the focused `Back` button,
  extends Roomers detail speech with clue text and empty-state text,
  extends Date A Dex detail speech with voice actor, likes, dislikes, pronouns, list summary, collectable count, and recipe text when visible, but only treats the entry and recipe panes as detail-visible so opening an entry resets stale list-state caching and should announce the bio immediately,
  filters Date A Dex bio fields against `DescScroll` so it only reads text blocks that are actually visible on screen instead of the full off-screen entry payload,
  more specifically, Date A Dex now clips the long `Desc` text to visible lines in the scroll viewport, while reading active side fields like voice actor, likes, dislikes, and pronouns normally,
  filters inactive TMP text out of generic UI extraction and gives Date A Dex its own selection handling so list entries announce their real visible labels instead of hidden placeholder text, while entry and recipe screens suppress focus chatter and defer to the detail announcer,
  also uses Date A Dex entry or recipe focus itself as a trigger to speak the current detail immediately and sync the detail cache so the separate detail poll does not repeat it,
  and now gives Date A Dex controls explicit labels such as Collectables, Sort, Recipe, Show Bio, and Back instead of reading only raw child text like `3 / total`,
  and applies a short Date A Dex focus-suppression window after a new bio or recipe detail announcement so the focused control does not immediately interrupt the content speech,
  and now debounces new Date A Dex detail text for a short settle window before speaking it, while suppressing focus speech whenever a new unsaid detail is pending,
  reads only the visible chat transcript window from `ParallelChat.Chatbox` using the `ScrollRect` viewport, removes the old latest-message fallback, and reads visible options from `ParallelChat.Options`,
  and announces art with the selected entry number plus title,
  and treats save/load focus as its own context so the new-save entry uses the `newSaveSlot` label path while existing saves announce parent `SaveSlot` metadata instead of the focused `Save` or `Load` button text.
- Latest SPECS regression fix:
  active `UIDialog` overlays now suppress underlying SPECS announcements,
  dialog-focus suppression now checks the actual dialog root as well as dialog buttons,
  SPECS stat and glossary blocks are matched before the page-toggle button,
  and stats-page overview and stat-block focus both include the visible stat descriptions again.
- Latest SPECS fallback change:
  `BuildSelectionAnnouncement()` now gives active single-button `UIDialog` overlays their title/body text,
  and the SPECS page-toggle selection now appends the current stats or glossary content instead of speaking only `Return to stats` or `Open glossary`.
- Latest SPECS hardening change:
  single-button `UIDialog` selections now stay suppressed for the full life of the dialog instead of only for the short timer window,
  and the SPECS page-toggle fallback now matches only the exact toggle controls instead of broad descendant checks that could collapse unrelated SPECS focus to the toggle label.
- Save/load button-focus fix:
  slot action buttons now return explicit button labels for Delete, Load, and Save before falling back to slot metadata, so focusing Delete should no longer read the slot name.
- Latest follow-up patch from the runtime logs:
  single-button `UIDialog` alerts now announce their title and body even when automatic screen-text reading is off,
  save/load slot `Save` and `Load` button focus now falls back to the slot metadata instead of speaking only the button label,
  and SPECS `KeyButton` and auto-fallback button focus no longer reuse the full stats or glossary page overview, so that content should stay on the detail-announcement path instead of repeating on every button focus.
- Latest SPECS follow-up patch:
  SPECS stats and glossary detail announcements now speak even when general screen-text speech is disabled,
  and the SPECS auto-fallback button now uses an explicit page-aware label instead of inheriting arbitrary child text.
- Latest SPECS page-state fix:
  the watcher now reads `SpecStatMain.currentPage` directly instead of inferring the page from active glossary objects,
  so the opening SPECS announcement and the glossary or stats button labels should now follow the real current page.
- Saved app-message data stores chat structure and stitch history, but not a ready-made rendered transcript, so full chat speech should prefer the live `ParallelChat` UI and Ink state.
- Local compile verification worked earlier in this environment, but the latest deploy attempt on 2026-03-25 failed only because the game had `DateEverythingAccess.dll` locked in the plugins folder while the project itself still built before the copy step.
- Dateviators equip and charge announcements tested well in normal play.
- Room announcements are acceptable for now, but numeric cues are not very meaningful; add spatial navigation cues where possible.
- Nearby interactables seem acceptable for already scanned objects, but unseen or unspoken object handling still needs explicit testing.
- Nearby interactable naming now uses the object-facing label while `Save.GetDateStatus(...)` is `Unmet`, then switches to the resolved character name after the player has met that datable.
- Add richer coverage for more gameplay states and visible UI text.
- Music and art screens were not discoverable during the 2026-03-25 runtime pass, so their phone coverage remains unverified but is no longer blocking the confirmed watcher fixes.
- Latest Date A Dex focus fix:
  the `OpenEntry(int)` hook now starts the Date A Dex open-entry focus suppression window immediately so the first bio announcement is less likely to be interrupted by the focused entry or collectables control,
  and generic phone-app content speech now skips Date A Dex list-entry focus so selecting a datable in the list should speak only the datable name instead of stale placeholder entry text such as `Something's Wrong here`.
- Latest Date A Dex timing hardening:
  the open-entry suppression window now expands to the whole visible Date A Dex entry screen instead of only the original narrow button check,
  and its duration is now estimated from the spoken bio length so a long entry should finish before `Collectables` focus speech is allowed to interrupt it.
- Latest tracker patch from the Maggie and auto-walk logs:
  objective tracking now canonicalizes tutorial targets to a usable interactable within the same hierarchy before zone lookup,
  interactable zone lookup now has a bounded nearest-zone fallback for awkward model-child targets,
  and navigation now keeps the current path step until the player actually leaves that step's `FromZone` instead of rebuilding every poll.
- Latest stairs auto-walk fix:
  runtime transit zones like `stairsdown` are now treated as in-between spaces rather than path-refresh points,
  so auto-walk keeps the current graph step alive through the stairs and falls through to the destination graph zone instead of reporting blocked at the stair landing.
- Latest open-passage source-handoff recovery fix:
  source-side open-passage recovery now keeps a sticky per-step handoff progress floor instead of expanding the handoff distance on refresh,
  so `SourceHandoff` should keep the furthest progressive target it already reached rather than snapping straight to the destination crossing anchor after a timeout,
  and the currently deployed DLL hash is `10495A336768DE6F93A39B4FD2CBB059FA885FBFD14006FD13FE6FAD67E669E7`.
- Latest open-passage committed-target refactor:
  open-passage source and destination handoffs now use committed geometric chunk targets that persist across path refreshes,
  those chunk targets only advance when the player reaches them or recovery explicitly pushes them forward,
  geometric progress now measures from the same source guidance origin used by the handoff path,
  and the currently deployed DLL hash is `8F3F4A23753DDD71880586774D23496DFF51EC7499441BAC77F5A44AB41549A3`.
- Latest tracked-object auto-walk fix from the 2026-04-11 full imports:
  direct-object guidance now requires an exact runtime-zone match instead of any broader family-equivalent match,
  equivalent sibling subzones fall back to the tracked subzone anchor before object approach begins,
  same-zone tracked-object targeting now caches and prefers the player's connected local-navigation component when the normalized zone contains multiple disconnected components such as `hallway`,
  and the newly built and deployed local DLL hash is `56638BB64C46C3F2F7ADB484E09245239E068074D4CA68843764BB57C0ECF78D`.

- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: clarified that reliable pathing metadata for sound-guided navigation is the product goal. Auto-walk and sweeps are debugging tools to validate whether the mod has accurate pathing data, not the intended primary player experience.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: rationale for keeping auto-walk in scope is objective debugging, lower reliance on subjective human audio-cue reports, easier timing comparisons, and likely user demand because house navigation is not meant to be the game's challenge. Working assumption after grilling: auto-walk is a necessary validation and fallback feature, but the primary experience should still be immersive audio guidance where a blind player retains control.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: audio cue success cannot be accepted as proven while auto-walk fails unless there is a more reliable objective metadata validator. Current validation rule: separate the concepts of route metadata and auto-walk execution, but do not claim sound guidance works for a route that the objective probe cannot validate or explain.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: metadata validation must prove more than internal route consistency. It must include the data needed by the system to guide a real blind player through the presented game world, including movement blockers such as furniture where they affect route followability, and the data source must match what the game actually presents to the player.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: authoritative world-fidelity source should be hybrid, with runtime behavior as final authority and decompiled/extracted/exported data from the same game build as supporting evidence. Missing required data should be treated as data debt that triggers a new export/decompile/runtime investigation, not as permission to keep layering heuristic fallbacks.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: if successful navigation requires data that is missing, the next task is to determine how to obtain that data before doing more routing work. Once obtained, the system must actually consume it. Only if the required data cannot be obtained should a fallback design be considered, because successful navigation is a core project requirement.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: scene metadata has already been extracted, and prior live navmesh export attempts did not provide an authoritative navmesh. Current suspicion is therefore higher-level transition semantics, especially door side/threshold/push-through meaning, rather than raw scene metadata absence.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: do not let the current door focus hide open-passage debt. Most open-passage transitions pass, but historical outliers such as bathroom2 subzones, bedroom/dorian bedroom, dining room to piano room, kitchen tutorial, dorian trapdoor, hallway arma, and living room to hallway still matter because reliable navigation requires all transition classes to be fixed.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: working recommendation is a unified transition semantic model covering doors and open passages, not another door-only threshold patch. Doors need richer fields, but open-passage outliers also need explicit source approach, crossing proof, destination proof, accepted intermediate subzones, blocker-aware beacon points, and transition subtype classification.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: code shape supports building and validating the semantic contract before more runtime movement tuning. `NavigationGraph` already carries point fields such as source/destination approach and clear points, but `AccessibilityWatcher` still derives much of the traversal meaning across door/open-passage targeting, sweeps, recovery, and auto-walk. Next architecture work should clarify that contract instead of adding another frame-by-frame heuristic.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: any transition semantics report must be a bounded path to a working in-game system, not open-ended research. It should answer the next implementation question directly: which transition semantics are complete, which data is missing or contradictory, and what single code/data change should be made next.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: first semantic audit can start with current failures plus passing controls, but the whole house must be audited once the semantic model stabilizes because failure sets change after patches. Full open/door sweeps are acceptable feedback loops because they take only a few minutes. The live route audit hotkey (`Ctrl+Alt+Shift+F1`) currently takes hours and mostly fails, so it is not useful as the primary proof loop until lower-level transition root issues are reduced.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: working validation cadence for the next phase is full open sweep plus full door sweep after meaningful transition changes. Avoid the hours-long live route audit except for targeted samples or a later milestone after transition-level reliability improves. Once the semantic audit covers the whole house, it should become the static preflight before runtime sweeps.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: definition of done is knowing that the mod has all data needed to reliably lead the player from any accessible room in the house to any other accessible room, excluding locked areas that require acquired objects. This can be broken into stages, but the target is complete room-to-room navigation metadata and behavior, not merely better sweep totals. Parallel work is desirable when issues are unrelated and can be validated independently.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: parallel work is acceptable only with guardrails that prevent cross-lane regressions and confusion. Each lane needs a clear owner/scope, disjoint write targets where possible, shared transition semantic contract, build-hash-stamped artifacts, a fixed passing-control set, and full open/door sweep validation before claiming improvement.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: next implementation direction is both a transition semantic contract and a minimal audit. The contract defines the fields that route/audio/auto-walk should share; the audit proves whether current artifacts can populate those fields. Keep the first version narrow so it becomes runtime-usable data rather than a disconnected report.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: v1 transition semantic contract should be limited to fields that can be populated or proven missing from current artifacts: stable transition id, kind/subtype, source/destination rooms, accepted source/destination/intermediate zones, connector names and interaction/prerequisite state, semantic beacon roles (source approach, crossing proof, destination clear/approach), local-map reachability/component evidence for each beacon, sweep evidence, and classification (`complete`, `incomplete`, `contradictory`, `locked/unavailable`, `execution-only failure`, or `unknown`).
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: v1 semantic audit should produce runtime-usable JSON plus a short human summary. The JSON is the candidate data contract the mod can later consume; the summary is only for review and next-action selection.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: keep `navigation_graph.json` as the base route graph for now and generate a semantic overlay keyed by stable transition ids. This reuses existing work and limits risk; if the overlay proves useful and the old graph fields become redundant, merge or replace later.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: do as much validation as possible offline/static before runtime. Generate and audit the semantic overlay first from repo artifacts and extracted data; runtime should remain the final validation step where possible, not the first place basic data-contract issues are discovered.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: generate the first semantic overlay for all transitions, since existing graph/local-map artifacts already cover the whole house and a partial schema would add special cases. Keep the human summary focused on current failures, changed rows, and a small passing-control set so review stays bounded.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: the navigation tooling surface is now confusing enough that the next implementation should clarify order of operations. Prefer a single pipeline/wrapper or documented manifest that runs the existing export/build/import/inspect steps in order and then generates the semantic overlay, instead of adding another disconnected report script.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: recommended next implementation is a navigation artifact pipeline wrapper first, with the semantic overlay/audit as a named step in that pipeline. Suggested shape: `.\scripts\Update-NavigationArtifacts.ps1` with clear modes such as static generation, runtime sweep import, and semantic audit, so future work has one documented order of operations.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: pipeline default should use existing artifacts. Expensive static regeneration must be opt-in via an explicit switch; normal iteration should import requested runtime reports and/or run the semantic audit without rebuilding every upstream artifact.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: development artifact generation is not a player requirement. Assume other players have the same game data unless evidence shows otherwise. Required navigation data must either ship with the mod or be located/extracted automatically at mod runtime; players should not be expected to run repo scripts or generate artifacts from their own copy.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: once the semantic overlay exists and is runtime-consumed, package it with the mod by default like `navigation_graph.json` and `local_navigation_maps.generated.json`. Runtime may warn on incompatible game/build data, but players should not need to generate the overlay.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: do not rely on old navigation behavior as a meaningful fallback once the semantic overlay is required; the old behavior is the known failing path. Missing required semantic data should produce a clear navigation-unavailable/setup error, not silently route through broken legacy heuristics.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: if semantic data is missing for a transition, disable only routes that require that transition when possible. Routes with complete semantic data should remain usable. Any route involving an incomplete transition should announce a clear route-data-incomplete error rather than trying broken fallback navigation.
- Grill-me design note on 2026-04-28: release intent is not to ship partial house navigation. Route-level incomplete-data blocking is a development safety rule and diagnostic behavior; release readiness requires the accessible room-to-room graph to be complete except for intentionally locked/prerequisite-gated areas.

Public surface matches the handoff queue: `GraphLinks` (57 primary zones / pairwise links from `InNavigationGraph` + the export's `GraphLinks` array), `IsPrimaryZone(name)`, `ResolvePrimaryZone(name)` (handles known sub-state suffixes `_tutorial` `_1love` `_2friend` `_3hate` `_shelley` `_cake` `_ronald` via a primary×suffix product table built at load, plus trailing-digit stripping at query time when the base is itself a primary), `GetDoorMetadata(doorName)` (the 30 real doors with non-null `DoorComponent`, each carrying world `Position` + `Rotation` + the matched OcclusionPortal when present, and a primary-only `NearestPrimaryZones` filtered from the export's `NearestGraphZones`), `GetTeleporter(name)` (the single `CrawlspaceLadder` entry with authored `LocationDown`/`LocationUp` and Euler `TeleportIn`/`OutRotation` converted to `Quaternion`), and `BlockersInBounds(Bounds)` (linear scan of the 67 primitive blockers, schema-stable so task A's MeshCollider footprints will land without consumer changes). - Current session note (2026-05-17 task A — MeshCollider triangle slicing in `Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1`): Wired the existing `Read-UnityMeshAsset.ps1` parser into the blocker exporter. Discovery work first: the original AssetRipper dump at `D:\root\AssetRipper\1.3.12\extracted\Ripped\ExportedProject` is unusable for meshes — every MeshCollider `m_Mesh` reference resolves to the AssetRipper "unresolved external" sentinel GUID `0000000deadbeef15deadf00d0000000` with no `.asset` file on disk. The premium-build dump at `D:\root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\extracted\Ripped\ExportedProject` has real geometry: 3,731 `Mesh\*.asset` files, 0 sentinel GUIDs, 1,607 distinct mesh refs in the scene. Switched the script defaults to the premium dump (`-ScenePath` and the new `-MeshAssetRoot`). Unity layer audit of the 2,317 MeshColliders in scene resolved against `ProjectSettings\TagManager.asset` shows layer 0 (Default) = 1,596, layer 18 (Mirror) = 1, layer 31 (3DUI) = 720 — no `Ignore Raycast`-layer MeshColliders exist contrary to the handoff guess, so the new `-SkipMeshLayers` default is `@(18, 31)`. Pipeline added to the script: build a GUID→asset-path index over `Mesh\*.asset.meta` once per run, JSON-cached at `artifacts\navigation\mesh_guid_index.cache.json` and invalidated by latest `.meta` mtime; resolve each MeshCollider's `m_Mesh` GUID via the index; transform mesh vertices into world space using the existing primitive-collider transform helpers; slice triangles at world-Y planes `PlayerSliceLowY=0.6` and `PlayerSliceHighY=1.5` via standard triangle-plane intersection (XZ-projected segment per crossed edge); fall back to vertex projection when the mesh sits inside the band but neither slice plane clips it; build an Andrew monotone-chain 2D convex hull; reject any hull whose `AreaSqM > MaxMeshFootprintAreaSqM=25.0` (catches floors / ceilings that survive the band filter), plus the existing per-record filters (`DisabledCollider`, `TriggerCollider`, `InactiveGameObject`, `DoorConnector`, `TeleporterConnector`, `RigidbodyObject`, `TinyFootprint`, `BelowBlockingHeight`, `AbovePlayerBand`, `MissingFootprintBounds`). Output schema: existing `PrimitiveColliders` and `NavigationBlockers` arrays unchanged for primitives; new top-level `MeshColliders` array holds full mesh records; `NavigationBlockers` now contains both primitives and mesh entries. Mesh footprints use a new `Kind="ConvexHull2D"` with `Center`, `Vertices` array of `{x,z}`, `AreaSqM`, and `IntersectedTriangleCount`; each record also exposes the standard `Bounds2D` axis-aligned summary so `SimpleNavSceneData.BlockersInBounds` keeps working without a runtime change. `Filtering.MeshCollidersUnsupported` now reports `false`; added `MeshSlicePlanes`, `MaxMeshFootprintAreaSqM`, `SkipMeshLayers` to `Filtering`; added `MeshCollidersResolved`, `MeshCollidersUnresolved`, `MeshColliderRecords`, `MeshGuidIndexEntries` to `Counts`; added `MeshColliderLayerHistogram`, `IgnoredMeshColliderCounts`, `MeshParseFailures`. Bugs fixed during validation: (1) `Where-Object` over a 2-element array returning `$null` under `Set-StrictMode -Version Latest` — switched to `@(...).Where({...})` for the slice-plane intersection check; (2) `[Parameter(Mandatory=$true)]` rejecting empty `List[Vector2]` arguments — added `[AllowEmptyCollection()]` to `Get-ConvexHull2D`, `Get-PolygonArea`, and `Get-TrianglePlaneIntersections`; (3) multiline-join regex over the YAML section lines was unreliable for the `m_Mesh:` capture — replaced with a per-line `Get-LineValue` lookup. Final end-to-end run: 2,317 scene MeshColliders → 1,551 with real `m_Mesh` references (766 carry `m_Mesh: {fileID: 0}` — null mesh, runtime no-op, correctly ignored as `MissingMeshReference`) → 1,475 resolved against the GUID index (76 `MeshGuidUnresolved`, 12 `MeshParseFailed` for meshes whose vertex stride isn't integral — fixable later by widening the parser, but a small share) → 365 footprint records produced after layer / disabled / trigger / door / teleporter / rigidbody / inactive filters → 238 surviving as navigation blockers after area / size filters (`EmptyFootprint`=1,016 mostly tall/thin objects spanning the band without crossing either slice plane, `FootprintAreaExceedsMax`=101, `TinyFootprint`=26). Combined blocker count: `67` primitives + `238` mesh = `305` total `NavigationBlockers` in `artifacts\navigation\thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json`. Outstanding follow-ups for the integration milestone or a refinement pass: (a) widen `Read-UnityMeshAsset.ps1` to handle non-integral-stride meshes (12 currently fail) and UInt32 indices; (b) consider a third slice plane around `y=1.0` to reduce the `EmptyFootprint`=1,016 rate for objects centered on the player's torso; (c) `SimpleNavBridge.SteerAroundObstacles` consumes a SphereCast today — `ConvexHull2D` footprints land via `Bounds2D` for now and only need a real hull-aware avoidance path once obstacle steering becomes load-bearing. Verification artifacts on disk: `artifacts\navigation\thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json` (3.7 MB), `artifacts\navigation\mesh_guid_index.cache.json`, `artifacts\navigation\exporter-run.log`. No build needed — script-only change. Task A is complete; the C→A→B handoff is closed and the integration milestone (flip `Navigation.UseSimpleNavigation` on, wire `SimpleNavBridge` into autowalk, rerun door + open-passage sweeps) is the next live task.

## Navigation redesign handoff (C → A → B)

User-approved direction (2026-05-16): stop patching the legacy stack and integrate the new `SimpleNav` module instead. Foundation is in repo; legacy stack remains the default until `Navigation.UseSimpleNavigation` is flipped on in config.

### Current state of the new module

- `SimpleNav.cs` — live `CameraSpaces` zones, live `Door` discovery, per-zone floor-Y cache, polymorphic `HasArrived`, `TryResolveTarget` (resolves once per step, not per frame). No graph file, no rasterized grid.
- `SimpleNavBridge.cs` — opt-in glue. Per-frame `Observe()` to sample floor Y. `TryGetTarget(stepKey, toZone)` caches the target for the duration of a step. `SteerAroundObstacles(playerPos, desiredDir)` does a short-range `Physics.SphereCast` and biases the autowalk direction laterally when blocked.
- `ModConfig.UseSimpleNavigation` — `Navigation/UseSimpleNavigation` in `BepInEx/config`, default `false`.
- `AccessibilityWatcher.Update` — single new line `SimpleNavBridge.Tick();` so `Observe()` runs each frame when the flag is on.

Build verified `0 warnings / 0 errors`. **Nothing yet consumes the bridge at the autowalk steering layer** — the legacy autowalk runs unchanged. Flipping the config flag today only enables floor-Y observation; it does not change movement.

### Asset-export inventory (decisions made this session)

- `thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json` is rich and stable. Useful fields: `GraphLinks` (56 pre-computed zone adjacencies), `CameraSpaces.InNavigationGraph` (57 of 169 zones are real rooms; rest are `dorian_*` / `_tutorial` / `_1love` virtual sub-zones), 30 `DoorObjects` with `DoorComponent`, 1 `Teleporters` entry (crawlspace ladder with authored `LocationDown`/`LocationUp` and `TeleportInRotation`/`TeleportOutRotation`).
- `thirdpersongreybox-blockers.json` covers 67 primitive `NavigationBlockers` but **skips 2,317 MeshColliders** (`MeshCollidersUnsupported: true`). User confirmed the original skip-meshes decision was driven by a valid concern: AABB-based mesh inclusion would block walkable space (floors, ceilings, L-shape negative space). Existing `BottomY`/`TopY` band filter (0.35–2.35) already handles floors/ceilings; the remaining problem is solvable by slicing mesh triangles at player chest height and projecting only intersecting triangles to XZ for a real footprint.
- Raw AssetRipper export at `D:\Root\AssetRipper\1.3.12\extracted\Ripped\ExportedProject\Assets\ThirdPersonGreybox.unity` (46 MB, 1.75M lines) contains everything else, notably `!u!41 OcclusionPortal` (16 entries) — exact 3D doorway openings for about half the doors, which would replace the swing-arc heuristics in `SimpleNav.TryResolveDoorApproach`.
- Both exporters (`scripts\Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1`, `scripts\Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1`) are ours and extensible.

### Ordered next-session queue

**(C) Extend `Export-SceneNavigationData.ps1` to emit `OcclusionPortals`.** Smallest, most contained. Add type ID `!u!41` to the parser, emit a top-level `OcclusionPortals` array with each entry: world `Center`, world `Size`, and the `GameObjectId` of the parent `Door` where resolvable. Regenerate `thirdpersongreybox-navigation-data.json`. Then in `SimpleNav.TryResolveDoorApproach`, use the portal bounds as the doorway opening when available, falling back to the current lateral-sidestep heuristic when absent.

**(A) Extend `Export-SceneBlockerData.ps1` to handle MeshColliders.** **DONE 2026-05-17.** Wired `Read-UnityMeshAsset.ps1` into the exporter, switched defaults to the premium AssetRipper dump (`D:\root\AssetRipper\1.3.12-premium\...`) because the original dump's MeshCollider refs all resolved to AssetRipper's `deadbeef` sentinel GUID. GUID→asset index is built once per run and JSON-cached. Triangles slice at world-Y `0.6` and `1.5`, project to XZ, convex hull, area-cap at `25 m²`. Layer audit found layer 18 (Mirror, 1 collider) and layer 31 (3DUI, 720 colliders) — both now in `-SkipMeshLayers`; no `Ignore Raycast` MeshColliders exist. Final blocker count grew from `67` primitives-only to `305` total (`67` primitives + `238` mesh). Mesh records emit `Kind="ConvexHull2D"` footprints alongside the standard `Bounds2D`, so `SimpleNavSceneData.BlockersInBounds` still works without a runtime change. See the 2026-05-17 session note above for full numbers, filter breakdown, and follow-ups (non-integral-stride mesh parsing, potential third slice plane, hull-aware obstacle steering).

**(B) Write `SimpleNavSceneData.cs`** consuming the richer exports. Loads once at startup, exposes:
- `IReadOnlyList<(string from, string to)> GraphLinks` for multi-zone BFS routing.
- `bool IsPrimaryZone(string zoneName)` from `InNavigationGraph` — authoritative answer to `HasArrived` ("did the player enter a real room?") that supersedes the current trailing-digit `ZoneFamily` heuristic.
- `DoorMetadata GetDoorMetadata(string doorName)` for the 30 real doors with `DoorComponent` + matched `OcclusionPortal` bounds.
- `TeleporterMetadata GetTeleporter(string name)` with authored target positions/rotations.
- `IEnumerable<StaticBlocker> BlockersInBounds(Bounds)` for per-zone obstacle queries.

Wire `SimpleNav.FindDoorBetween` to consult `GraphLinks` first (graph lookup) and fall back to live AABB-distance only if metadata is missing. Replace `SimpleNav.IsInZoneFamily`'s trailing-digit heuristic with `SimpleNavSceneData.IsPrimaryZone` + alias lookup.

### Integration milestone (after C+A+B)

Hook `SimpleNavBridge.TryGetTarget` and `SteerAroundObstacles` into the autowalk movement-vector computation in `AccessibilityWatcher` so flipping `UseSimpleNavigation = true` actually moves the player using the new stack. Then run the door + open-passage sweeps with the flag on, compare to the legacy 13 + 17 failure baseline. Once the new stack passes the sweep, retire `LocalNavigationMaps`, the legacy per-frame `TryGetNextNavigationPosition`, the door state-machine recovery layers, and the seven untracked design docs (`NAVIGATION_REVIEW.md`, `NAVIGATION_FAILURE_TRIAGE.md`, `NEXT_STEPS.md`, `TEST_VALIDATION_HYPOTHESIS.md`, `VALIDATION_INTEGRATION_GUIDE.md`, plus `NavigationTargetValidation.cs` and `NavigationValidationTestHarness.cs`).

### Honest caveats carried forward

- Obstacle avoidance in `SimpleNavBridge.SteerAroundObstacles` is local steering, not a planner. Assumes the target is on the same side of any wall as the player; SimpleNav routing through door-approach points has to guarantee that.
- Door-clearance lateral sidestep is currently a 0.5m constant. Reflection hooks for `Door.startRot`/`Door.currentRot` are in place but unused; analytic swing-arc math replaces the constant once `OcclusionPortal` data is available for the door (preferred) or once we wire `startRot` + `range` (fallback).
- Floor-Y for a zone requires the player to have entered it at least once. Cold-boot navigation to a never-visited zone uses a fallback Y from the closest sampled sibling zone.
